Learning Path 02

Apparaten beheren en onderhouden

4 modules · Nederlands met Engelstalige cursusverdieping

Learning Path 02Module 01

Configuratieprofielen en beleidsbeheer

Je maakt configuratie- en compliancebeleid, kiest doelgroepen en onderzoekt conflicten.

← Vorige moduleVolgende module →↑ Terug naar boven

Waarom is deze module belangrijk?

Een apparaat kan meerdere beleidsbronnen ontvangen. Duidelijke scope en eigenaarschap voorkomen onvoorspelbare instellingen.

Leerdoelen

  • Uitleggen: configureren tegenover evalueren.
  • Uitleggen: profielen en toewijzing.
  • Uitleggen: gPO’s analyseren en migreren.
  • Uitleggen: conflicten oplossen.

01. Configureren tegenover evalueren

Configuratiebeleid stelt gewenste waarden in, bijvoorbeeld voor Wi-Fi of versleuteling. Compliancebeleid evalueert of een apparaat aan eisen voldoet. Conditional Access gebruikt zulke signalen voor toegang. Een noncompliant status activeert op zichzelf geen BitLocker-configuratie: de herstelmaatregel moet de onderliggende instelling daadwerkelijk aanpassen.

02. Profielen en toewijzing

Gebruik Settings catalog, beschikbare templates of een ondersteund custom profiel. Kies eerst platform en gebruikers- of apparaatscope. Leg per instelling doel, eigenaar en testgroep vast. Groepen selecteren de doelgroep; filters verfijnen op apparaatkenmerken. Controleer include- en exclude-logica bij iedere uitbreiding.

03. GPO’s analyseren en migreren

Group Policy analytics helpt ondersteunde en niet rechtstreeks migreerbare instellingen te herkennen. Exporteer de relevante GPO-informatie, analyseer de ondersteuning en maak een gefaseerd migratieplan. Een technische match bewijst nog niet dat de beleidsintentie behouden blijft. Test het resultaat voordat je lokale policies terugtrekt.

04. Conflicten oplossen

Lees de status per instelling en identificeer alle bronnen die dezelfde instelling beheren. Consolidatie of gerichte uitsluiting is vaak beter dan een extra profiel toevoegen. Ga niet uit van één universele voorrangsregel voor alle CSP’s en beleidssoorten. Verifieer de effectieve waarde op het apparaat en de rapportage na synchronisatie.

Wat moet je als beheerder onthouden?

  • Configuratie stelt in; compliance beoordeelt.
  • Ontwerp één duidelijke beleidsverantwoordelijke per instelling.
  • Controleer ondersteuning voordat je een GPO migreert.

Samenvatting

Configuratie stelt in; compliance beoordeelt. Ontwerp één duidelijke beleidsverantwoordelijke per instelling. Controleer ondersteuning voordat je een GPO migreert. Gebruik de hoofdstukken om keuzes te onderbouwen en controleer altijd de effectieve toestand naast de portalstatus.

Cursusverdieping per onderwerp (Engels) · 14 bronslides

Original course content. Text order follows the source presentation; visual tables and diagrams are represented as text.

Slide 5

Understand device configuration profiles and policy enforcement

  • Configuration profiles
  • Reusable settings assigned to devices or users : delivered automatically on enrollment
  • What they are
  • Reusable collections of settings defined once, assigned to devices or users
  • Delivered automatically on enrollment : BitLocker, password rules, Wi-Fi
  • Operate independently from compliance policies
  • How they enforce
  • Deliver settings directly to devices for consistency
  • System-level settings apply immediately; user-level apply at sign-in
  • Eliminates manual configuration across the fleet
  • Why they matter
  • The backbone of Intune device management
  • Enforce security across thousands of devices
  • Meet requirements from enrollment; cut manual effort
  • Key insight
  • Profiles configure proactively; compliance policies verify adherence reactively. Together they deliver complete policy enforcement : e.g. one profile delivers Contoso's 14-character password rule and Wi-Fi.
Slide 6

Configuration profile types, scope, and conflicts

  • Device profiles
  • Apply to the hardware, regardless of user
  • Wi-Fi, VPN, encryption, camera, Bluetooth
  • User profiles
  • Follow the user across devices
  • Email accounts, app settings, user restrictions
  • Admin templates
  • Group Policy power for cloud-managed Windows
  • Hundreds of settings, no on-prem infrastructure
  • Custom profiles
  • OMA-URI (Windows) or Plist (Apple)
  • For settings not in standard types : needs expertise
  • Scope, inheritance & conflict resolution
  • Assign to security groups, device groups, or individual users. Multiple profiles can layer on one device. Conflicting values surface a Conflict state for manual resolution : in most cases the last-applied profile wins.
Slide 7

Create configuration profiles

  • Create configuration profiles
  • Templates · Settings catalog · Custom profiles
  • Templates
  • Settings organized by category : security, connectivity, system
  • Use when requirements fit standard buckets (firewall, antivirus, device restrictions)
  • Example: device restriction + endpoint protection for Windows 11
  • Settings catalog
  • Search by name across thousands of settings
  • Combine specific settings into one lean profile
  • Best when templates don't cover the scenario
  • Custom profiles
  • Deploy settings outside standard options via OMA-URI (Windows) or Plist (Apple)
  • Require exact syntax : higher risk
  • Reserve for settings not exposed in standard types
  • Key insight
  • Match the approach to the requirement: templates for the familiar, the settings catalog for the specific, and custom profiles only when neither delivers what you need.
Slide 8

Profile creation workflow and structure

  • 1 Choose platform
  • Windows · macOS · iOS · iPadOS · Android
  • 2 Pick template or catalog
  • Or a custom profile
  • 3 Configure settings
  • Define the configuration
  • 4 Assign to groups
  • Target users or device groups
  • 5 Monitor application
  • Track status across the fleet
  • Every profile = three elements
  • Settings : define the configuration
  • Assignments : target users or groups
  • Filters : refine by device name, manufacturer, or OS version
Slide 9

Assign configuration profiles using groups and filters

  • Groups set the primary audience. Filters refine that audience by device properties : so each profile reaches exactly the right devices and users.
  • Static user & device groups
  • Membership managed manually in Microsoft Entra ID
  • Best for stable teams or known device sets that rarely change
  • Dynamic groups
  • Rules on attributes : department, OS version, manufacturer
  • Members added or removed automatically as attributes change : no upkeep
  • All users · All devices
  • Broad assignment for mandatory baselines (firewall, Windows Update)
  • Reserve for universal, foundational policies only
  • When to use which
  • User groups → user settings (Wi-Fi, email, VPN). Device groups → encryption, naming, Windows Updates. All users / devices → universal baselines. Combine user groups for experience and device groups for hardware.
Slide 10

Refine profile assignments with filters

  • Filters carry technical criteria : layer device properties on top of a group without creating new groups.
  • Device properties
  • Manufacturer & model : Dell, HP, Lenovo, Apple; Surface Pro, MacBook, iPad
  • OS version : Windows 11 23H2, iOS 17
  • Ownership : Corporate vs Personal, plus custom device categories
  • 1 Include filters narrow
  • Assign broadly, then narrow : e.g. ownership = Corporate on All Devices
  • 2 Exclude filters remove
  • Push a policy to all Windows, exclude the QA fleet (category = Test)
  • 3 Real-time evaluation
  • Filters check properties on every check-in; targeting adjusts automatically
  • 4 Applicability vs assignment
  • Applies only when criteria are met and the platform supports the settings
  • Combining strategy
  • Groups carry business logic (departments, regions, roles). Filters carry technical criteria (ownership, model, OS). Pilot against a small group first, verify per device, then expand.
Slide 11

Create compliance policies

  • Define standards
  • Pick platform · set rules
  • Configure settings
  • Three categories per policy
  • Monitor
  • Compliance rate · failures · status
  • Device health
  • Antivirus active
  • BitLocker / FileVault on
  • Secure boot enabled
  • Minimum OS version
  • Device & system security
  • Strong passwords and firewall
  • TPM presence
  • Jailbreak / root detection
  • Conditional Access
  • Intune reports status to Entra ID
  • Non-compliant devices blocked from Teams, SharePoint, Exchange
Slide 12

Build, assign, and monitor a compliance policy

  • 1 Pick platform
  • Windows · macOS · iOS · Android
  • 2 Set grace period
  • Admin window to remediate (e.g. 30 days)
  • 3 Assign group
  • Microsoft Entra ID security group
  • Compliance outcomes
  • Compliant : meets every rule
  • Non-compliant : notify, then enforce
  • Monitor
  • Compliant rate across the fleet
  • Top failures to prioritize
  • Per-device compliance status
  • Build in the Intune admin center → Devices. The same three-category settings (device health, security, Conditional Access) define every policy.
Slide 13

Assign compliance policies using groups and filters

  • Groups deliver the policy to the right users or devices. Filters add precision at assignment time.
  • Static groups
  • Membership managed by hand in Microsoft Entra ID
  • Best for finance, legal, executives or named device sets that rarely change
  • Dynamic groups
  • Rules on OS version, ownership, enrollment type
  • Policies stay assigned automatically as devices join or leave
  • All users · All devices
  • Broad targeting for org-wide baselines (minimum OS, encryption)
  • Reserve for universal compliance rules
  • When to use which
  • User groups → policy evaluates on every device the user signs into. Device groups → static targeting regardless of who signs in. All users / devices → baseline security only.
Slide 14

Refine compliance assignments with filters

  • Filters add device-level precision : handle edge cases without overlapping groups.
  • Device properties
  • Ownership : Corporate vs Personal
  • Manufacturer & model : e.g. exclude Surface Go, Windows Home
  • OS version & join type : Entra joined, hybrid joined, specific OS
  • 1 Include filters narrow
  • Device must meet every include : e.g. ownership = Corporate AND OS = Windows
  • 2 Exclude filters remove
  • Strict policy on corporate Windows, but skip model = Surface Go
  • 3 Evaluation order
  • Group → includes → excludes; one exclude match drops the device
  • 4 Cleaner than extra groups
  • Handle edge cases with one clean assignment
  • Combining strategy
  • Groups map to divisions, regions, or roles. Filters layer ownership, model, or OS edition. Pilot, document, then expand across the estate.
Slide 15

Analyze and migrate Group Policy with analytics

  • Group Policy analytics
  • Scan on-prem GPOs → a data-driven Intune migration plan
  • Three categories: Supported · Partially supported · Not supported
  • Supported
  • Direct Intune equivalents : one-to-one mappings
  • Migrate first : quick wins with full parity that prove the approach
  • Partially supported
  • Map with reduced scope (GPO covers 10 aspects, Intune 6)
  • Report calls out the specific differences + Microsoft's recommended approach
  • Not supported
  • No cloud equivalent : deprecated or on-prem only
  • Options: custom OMA-URI, an alternative control, or accept it won't apply
Slide 16

Migrate Group Policy in phases

  • 1 Export GPO backups
  • From GPMC (Group Policy Management Console)
  • 2 Upload to analytics
  • Group Policy analytics in the Intune admin center
  • 3 Review the report
  • Setting level (each tagged) + GPO level (overall readiness); filter to focus
  • Phased migration
  • Phase 1 : migrate supported security GPOs
  • Phase 2 : plan workarounds for partial
  • Phase 3 : redesign or retire not-supported
  • Tip: start with your most critical security GPOs
  • Why it matters
  • Analytics gives the full migration scope before you start, preserves years of policy decisions, and makes stakeholder conversations evidence-based. Phased migration cuts risk and rework.
Slide 17

Troubleshoot device configuration and policy application

  • Visibility tells you what failed. A workflow tells you why.
  • Policy status values
  • Success : applied cleanly
  • Error : needs investigation
  • Pending : awaiting sync; allow time or force
  • Not applicable : wrong platform (not a failure)
  • Conflict : two policies set the same setting differently
  • Where to look
  • 1 Policy-level overview
  • Devices → Configuration profiles / Compliance policies : counts per status
  • 2 Per-device drill-down
  • Device status or User status : which devices or users failed
  • 3 Per-setting status
  • Open one device : exactly which settings applied or failed
Slide 18

Diagnose and fix policy failures

  • Root causes
  • Scoping : wrong group or filter excludes the device
  • Eligibility : not enrolled, non-compliant, or no license
  • Sync delay : offline or stale check-in; force a sync
  • Conflict : two policies set different values
  • 1 Verify the assignment
  • Confirm group membership; check filters vs device attributes
  • 2 Check device eligibility
  • Enrollment, compliance, license, platform : then Sync
  • 3 Troubleshooting + Support
  • Inspect sign-ins, license, assignments, sync; watch for error codes
  • 4 Resolve and re-test
  • Adjust groups, refine filters, remove conflicts; confirm Success
  • Field tips: sync before you escalate · inspect from both angles (policy view vs device view) · resolve conflicts at the source.

Kies per vraag één antwoord. Je kunt opnieuw proberen; de score bepaalt je modulevoortgang niet.

1. Je wilt BitLocker actief inschakelen. Welk beleid is nodig?

2. Twee profielen stellen dezelfde VPN-instelling verschillend in. Wat is de juiste aanpak?

Oorspronkelijke kennisvragen uit de cursus (Engels)

Unchanged question wording from the source decks, apart from typography. Answers and explanations, where present, come from the associated speaker notes. Treat them as course-source answers; validate current product behavior in Microsoft documentation.

Slide 19 · Question 1

You need to configure BitLocker encryption on 500 Windows devices. You want the encryption settings to apply regardless of which user signs in. Which type of profile should you use?

  1. Device profile
  2. User profile
  3. Administrative template
Answer and source explanation

Q1 - Correct: A. Device profile Correct. Device profiles apply settings to the device itself, so settings are enforced regardless of which user signs in. Since BitLocker is a device-level security setting, a device profile is the appropriate choice. B. Incorrect. User profiles apply settings based on the signed-in user and follow that user across devices. BitLocker encryption must be applied at the device level and shouldn't vary by user. C. Incorrect. Administrative templates configure specific policy settings (similar to Group Policy), but they don't determine whether settings are scoped to the device or user. For device-level enforcement regardless of user, a device profile is required.

Slide 19 · Question 2

Sales representatives at Contoso use multiple devices as they travel and need the same wireless network configuration on every device they use. Which profile type best supports this requirement?

  1. Device profile
  2. User profile
  3. Administrative template
Answer and source explanation

Q2 - Correct: B. User profile Correct. User profiles apply settings based on the user identity and follow the user across multiple devices. This ensures sales representatives receive the same wireless configuration on any device they sign in to. A. Incorrect. Device profiles apply settings to the device regardless of the user. Since the requirement is that the wireless configuration follows the user across multiple devices, a user profile is the correct choice. C. Incorrect. Administrative templates configure specific settings but are typically device-scoped. They don't follow a user across multiple devices the way a user profile does.

Slide 19 · Question 3

You assigned a Windows Defender configuration profile to a security group, but some devices show a 'Pending' status after 24 hours. You've verified the devices belong to the correct group. What should you check first?

  1. Device platform compatibility with the profile settings
  2. Device enrollment status and Intune connectivity
  3. Filter logic in the profile assignment
Answer and source explanation

Q3 - Correct: B. Device enrollment status and Intune connectivity Correct. Pending status typically means the device hasn't checked in with Intune to receive the profile. Verify that devices are properly enrolled, have network connectivity, and have synced recently. Offline or recently enrolled devices will show pending until the next sync. A. Incorrect. Platform compatibility issues typically result in an 'Error' status, not 'Pending.' Pending status indicates the device hasn't yet communicated with Intune to receive the profile. C. Incorrect. Assignment filter issues would cause the device to be excluded from the assignment entirely rather than show a pending status. Pending means the profile was assigned but not yet received by the device.

Slide 20 · Question 1

You've assigned two profiles to the same Windows devices: Profile A enables Windows Defender, and Profile B configures wireless network settings. What happens when both profiles are received?

  1. Devices apply settings from both profiles
  2. Devices apply only Profile A because it configures a security setting
  3. A conflict is generated and neither profile applies
Answer and source explanation

Q4 - Correct: A. Devices apply settings from both profiles Correct. Intune supports layering multiple profiles on the same device. Since the two profiles configure different settings (antivirus vs. wireless), there's no conflict and both profiles apply successfully. B. Incorrect. Intune doesn't prioritize profiles based on their content type. Both profiles are applied independently, and security settings don't take precedence over other configuration settings. C. Incorrect. Conflicts only occur when two profiles configure the same setting with different values. Since these profiles target different settings entirely, both apply without conflict.

Slide 20 · Question 2

Your organization is planning to migrate on-premises Group Policy settings to Microsoft Intune. You need to determine which GPO settings are supported before creating configuration profiles. Which Intune feature should you use?

  1. Settings catalog
  2. Group Policy analytics
  3. Device restriction template
Answer and source explanation

Q5 - Correct: B. Group Policy analytics Correct. Group Policy analytics analyzes imported on-premises GPO settings and reports which ones are supported in Intune's cloud-based policies. This is essential for planning a GPO migration and identifying settings that require alternative approaches. A. Incorrect. The settings catalog lets you browse and configure available Intune settings, but it doesn't analyze your existing on-premises GPOs or identify which settings can be migrated. C. Incorrect. Device restriction templates configure specific device settings but don't analyze or evaluate existing Group Policy objects. They're a destination for some migrated settings, not a migration analysis tool.

Learning Path 02Module 02

Apparaten monitoren en onderhouden

Je combineert inventaris, Endpoint analytics en Remediations om de gezondheid van endpoints te bewaken.

← Vorige moduleVolgende module →↑ Terug naar boven

Waarom is deze module belangrijk?

Een succesvolle beleidsuitrol zegt weinig over trage starts of terugkerende problemen die gebruikers dagelijks ondervinden.

Leerdoelen

  • Uitleggen: bruikbare signalen verzamelen.
  • Uitleggen: endpoint analytics.
  • Uitleggen: detectie en herstel.
  • Uitleggen: rapporten en automatisering.

01. Bruikbare signalen verzamelen

Begin bij inventaris, laatste check-in, configuratiestatus en compliance. Scheid ontbrekende of verouderde data van bevestigde fouten. Een offline apparaat kan geen recente beleidsstatus terugmelden. Bepaal een eigenaar, drempel en opvolgactie voor ieder rapport, zodat monitoring tot een beheersbare werklijst leidt.

02. Endpoint analytics

Endpoint analytics helpt opstartprestaties en applicatiebetrouwbaarheid te vergelijken. Kijk naar trends, aantallen en getroffen modellen voordat je conclusies trekt uit een gemiddelde. Vergelijk dezelfde doelgroepen over een gelijke meetperiode. Controleer vereisten en aanvullende licenties voor geavanceerde mogelijkheden.

03. Detectie en herstel

Remediations combineert een detectiescript met een herstelscript. Bij deze functie betekent exitcode 1 van de detectie dat een probleem werd gevonden en herstel nodig is. Controleer uitvoeringscontext, architectuur en planning. Zorg dat herstel herhaalbaar is en rapporteer zowel een mislukte detectie als een mislukte correctie.

04. Rapporten en automatisering

Gebruik gerichte inventarisexports voor OS-versies en check-ininformatie. Volg update- en beleidsrapporten voor de bijbehorende workload. Met Microsoft Graph kun je herhaald verzamelen automatiseren. Beperk toegang tot exports en leg vast wanneer de gegevens zijn verzameld; een momentopname is geen realtime toestand.

Wat moet je als beheerder onthouden?

  • Controleer de actualiteit van meetgegevens.
  • Gebruik een detectie vóór automatische correctie.
  • Meet het effect op de gebruikerservaring.

Samenvatting

Controleer de actualiteit van meetgegevens. Gebruik een detectie vóór automatische correctie. Meet het effect op de gebruikerservaring. Gebruik de hoofdstukken om keuzes te onderbouwen en controleer altijd de effectieve toestand naast de portalstatus.

Understand the role of monitoring in endpoint management

  • Profile / policy status: Succeeded, Error, Conflict, Pending, Not applicable
  • Act on insights

  • Sync devices to trigger a check-in and validate changes
  • Improve targeting, reduce conflicts, support remediation
  • Check-in cadence
  • Most enrolled devices check in about every 8 hours

Use Endpoint Analytics to monitor device health and performance

Monitor Windows Update Rings and feature update deployments

  • Prevents unintended version drift
  • Update ring

Kies per vraag één antwoord. Je kunt opnieuw proberen; de score bepaalt je modulevoortgang niet.

1. Waar onderzoek je een vloot met trage opstarttijden?

2. Wat betekent exitcode 1 in een Remediations-detectiescript?

Oorspronkelijke kennisvragen uit de cursus (Engels)

Unchanged question wording from the source decks, apart from typography. Answers and explanations, where present, come from the associated speaker notes. Treat them as course-source answers; validate current product behavior in Microsoft documentation.

Slide 30 · Question 1

Your organization's help desk receives frequent tickets from users saying their devices are slow to start up. You want to identify which devices are affected and understand the root cause before rolling out any fixes. Which Intune feature is most appropriate?

  1. Device compliance reports
  2. Endpoint Analytics
  3. Remediations
Answer and source explanation

Q1 - Correct: B. Endpoint Analytics Correct. Endpoint Analytics collects device telemetry and surfaces startup performance scores, boot time breakdowns, and app reliability data. It helps you identify which devices are affected and what factors (such as driver delays or startup apps) are contributing to slow startup. A. Incorrect. Compliance reports show whether devices meet policy requirements such as OS version or encryption. They don't provide startup performance data or help identify the root cause of slow boot times. C. Incorrect. Remediations are used to detect and fix known issues after they're identified. Endpoint Analytics is the right tool to first discover and understand the scope and cause of the startup performance problem.

Slide 30 · Question 2

A configuration profile shows a 'Conflict' status for 15 devices. You've confirmed the devices are enrolled and checking in. What is the most likely cause?

  1. The devices haven't synced with Intune since the profile was assigned
  2. Two or more assigned profiles are configuring the same setting with different values
  3. The profile platform doesn't match the device operating system
Answer and source explanation

Q2 - Correct: B. Two or more assigned profiles are configuring the same setting with different values Correct. A Conflict status means the device received multiple policies that configure the same setting with different values. Intune cannot reconcile the contradiction, so neither value is applied. You need to review overlapping profile assignments and resolve the conflicting settings. A. Incorrect. Devices that haven't synced would show a 'Pending' status, not 'Conflict'. Conflict indicates the device has received the policy but there's a contradiction in the applied settings. C. Incorrect. A platform mismatch typically results in the profile not being applicable to the device at all, rather than a Conflict status. Conflict specifically indicates competing settings from multiple applied policies.

Slide 30 · Question 3

You want to automatically detect and fix a recurring issue where a scheduled Windows Defender scan is disabled on devices after a software deployment. Which Intune feature should you use?

  1. Endpoint Analytics
  2. Compliance policies
  3. Remediations
Answer and source explanation

Q3 - Correct: C. Remediations Correct. Remediations deploy a detection script to check device state and a remediation script to fix the issue when it's detected. This is the right tool for automatically correcting recurring configuration drift, such as a disabled Defender scan, without requiring user or admin intervention. A. Incorrect. Endpoint Analytics is a monitoring and insights tool. It can help you identify that a problem exists and measure its scope, but it doesn't execute detection or remediation scripts on devices. B. Incorrect. Compliance policies assess device state and can mark non-compliant devices or trigger Conditional Access. They don't run scripts to detect and automatically correct specific configuration issues like a disabled scan schedule.

Slide 31 · Question 1

A security auditor asks for a report showing all enrolled devices, their OS versions, and their last check-in date. Where in the Intune admin center should you go?

  1. Devices > Monitor > Device compliance
  2. Devices > All devices → choose columns/filters → Export to CSV
  3. Endpoint Analytics > Overview
Answer and source explanation

Q4 - Correct: B. Devices > All devices → choose columns/filters → Export to CSV Correct. The Intune admin center doesn't provide a dedicated 'Device inventory' report under Reports. To produce an inventory-style export with device name, OS version, OS build, compliance state, and last check-in, open Devices > All devices, choose the columns and filters you need, and export the list to CSV. A. Incorrect. Devices > Monitor > Device compliance shows compliance state, not a full enrolled-device inventory with OS and check-in details. C. Incorrect. Endpoint Analytics provides performance and health scores, not an inventory list suitable for audit purposes.

Slide 31 · Question 2

Your team needs to export a daily list of all noncompliant devices and email it to the security team. Doing this manually in the portal takes 20 minutes each day. What is the best approach to automate this task?

  1. Configure a compliance policy notification to alert the security team when devices become noncompliant
  2. Use PowerShell with the Microsoft Graph API to query noncompliant devices and send a daily email
  3. Schedule a Remediation to run daily and output the list of noncompliant devices
Answer and source explanation

Q5 - Correct: B. Use PowerShell with the Microsoft Graph API to query noncompliant devices and send a daily email Correct. PowerShell combined with the Microsoft Graph API can query the Intune device compliance endpoint, filter for noncompliant devices, format the results, and send an automated email : making the process repeatable, consistent, and free from manual portal work. A. Incorrect. Compliance policy notifications send alerts per device as noncompliance is detected, but they don't produce a consolidated daily list. The security team would receive individual alerts rather than a single daily report. C. Incorrect. Remediations run detection and remediation scripts on individual devices, not across the organization to aggregate compliance data. They aren't designed to query tenant-wide device state and generate reports.

Learning Path 02Module 03

Windows-updates en apparaatlevenscyclus

Je organiseert kwaliteits- en functie-updates en kiest passende acties voor de apparaatlevenscyclus.

← Vorige moduleVolgende module →↑ Terug naar boven

Waarom is deze module belangrijk?

Te snelle uitrol kan bedrijfsprocessen verstoren; te late uitrol vergroot de periode waarin kwetsbaarheden aanwezig blijven.

Leerdoelen

  • Uitleggen: updatebeleid verdelen.
  • Uitleggen: kanalen, Autopatch en Hotpatch.
  • Uitleggen: updatefouten onderzoeken.
  • Uitleggen: retire, wipe en reset.

01. Updatebeleid verdelen

Kwaliteitsupdates leveren onderhoud en beveiligingscorrecties; functie-updates veranderen de Windows-release. Update rings regelen onder meer uitstel, deadlines en herstarts. Feature update policies richten zich op de gewenste release. Stem deze mechanismen op elkaar af en vermijd overlappende beleidsbronnen.

02. Kanalen, Autopatch en Hotpatch

Gebruik een beperkte validatiegroep en breid uit op basis van bewijs. Windows Autopatch automatiseert delen van de uitrol voor geschikte omgevingen. Hotpatch vermindert bepaalde herstarts op ondersteunde configuraties, maar verwijdert de noodzaak van alle herstarts niet. Controleer actuele editie-, release- en licentievereisten. LTSC is bedoeld voor passende gespecialiseerde scenario’s.

03. Updatefouten onderzoeken

Controleer bereikbaarheid, schijfruimte, ondersteunde release en toepasselijke policies. Houd rekening met safeguard holds en herstartstatus. Vergelijk rapportage met de clientstatus en logs. Pauzeer of wijzig een rollout op basis van aantoonbare impact en leg vast wie beslist wanneer de uitrol hervat.

04. Retire, wipe en reset

Retire verwijdert beheer en bepaalde zakelijke gegevens afhankelijk van het platform. Wipe kan gegevens destructief verwijderen. Reset-acties hebben verschillende doelen en voorwaarden, waaronder hergebruik van een beheerd Windows-apparaat. Controleer expliciet welke gegevens, joinrelaties en inschrijvingen behouden blijven bij de gekozen actie.

Wat moet je als beheerder onthouden?

  • Stem update rings af op de gewenste Windows-release.
  • Hotpatch betekent niet dat herstarts nooit nodig zijn.
  • Lees de gevolgen van iedere lifecycle-actie vóór uitvoering.

Samenvatting

Stem update rings af op de gewenste Windows-release. Hotpatch betekent niet dat herstarts nooit nodig zijn. Lees de gevolgen van iedere lifecycle-actie vóór uitvoering. Gebruik de hoofdstukken om keuzes te onderbouwen en controleer altijd de effectieve toestand naast de portalstatus.

Cursusverdieping per onderwerp (Engels) · 7 bronslides

Original course content. Text order follows the source presentation; visual tables and diagrams are represented as text.

Slide 34

Understand the Windows servicing model and update types

  • Balance speed & stability
  • Push security updates fast to a test ring first
  • Validate 1-2 weeks before broad rollout
  • Stage feature updates over weeks via rings
  • Pick a servicing channel
  • Current Channel : features ~twice a year
  • Enterprise LTSC : features every 2-3 years
  • Semi-Annual (deprecated) : being phased out
  • Choice drives cost, testing, support
  • Know the update types
  • Quality : monthly security & bug fixes
  • Feature : twice a year, larger changes
  • Preview : optional, validate first
  • Each type needs its own plan
  • Mind the servicing window
  • Each version has a support window
  • Deferring delays within that window
  • Upgrade before the window closes
  • e.g. Windows 10 21H2 ended June 13, 2023
  • Track end-of-service per device
Slide 35

Implement update rings and feature update policies in Intune

  • Pilot ring
  • 5% · 0-day deferral
  • Standard ring
  • 70% · 30-day deferral
  • Conservative ring
  • 25% · 90-day deferral
  • How update rings work
  • Stage feature updates in waves : each group gets them at a different time
  • Set a per-ring deferral : wait time after release before deploying
  • Create in Devices > Update rings for Windows 10 and later > Create profile
  • Overlapping rings → Intune applies the least restrictive deferral
  • Feature update policies pin a version; rings control timing : use both
  • Monitor each ring
  • Compliance : status per ring
  • Restarts : devices awaiting reboot
  • Failures : investigate before rolling out
Slide 36

Configure and manage Hotpatch with Microsoft Intune

  • 1 Patch in memory
  • Kernel & system components patched while Windows runs
  • 2 No restart
  • Sessions, apps, and work continue uninterrupted
  • 3 Ops continue
  • Servers, trading floors, medical & manufacturing stay online
  • 4 Persists at restart
  • The next scheduled restart writes the patch permanently
  • Eligibility checklist
  • Windows 11 Enterprise or Pro (Win 10 Pro with KB5028997+)
  • Intel or AMD CPU from 2016 or newer
  • VBS and Secure Boot enabled
  • Combine with quality deferral
  • Within a ring, set Hotpatch to 0 days for urgent fixes
  • Defer other quality updates for testing
  • Quality updates: 7-day deferral | Hotpatch updates: 0-day
  • Devices > Update rings (Win 11) > Hotpatch = Yes
Slide 37

Manage Windows servicing channels for enterprise devices

  • Current Channel
  • Feature updates twice a year
  • Best for modern apps, rapid adopters
  • Needs a quarterly test pipeline
  • Fits standard business devices
  • Preview Channel
  • Windows Insider builds
  • Validate features ahead of release
  • Use only on a small pilot set
  • Avoid for production
  • Enterprise LTSC
  • Feature updates every 2-3 years
  • Monthly security + quality continue
  • Medical, industrial, kiosk, legacy apps
  • LTSC 21H2 covered until Jan 2027
  • Assign & plan
  • Set via Autopilot or config profile
  • Channel can't change without reimage
  • Test new versions in 60-90 days
  • Pilot before broad transitions
Slide 38

Implement Windows Autopatch for automated updates

  • Windows Autopatch
  • Automates Windows + Microsoft 365 Apps updates end-to-end : no manual rings
  • Test
  • 1% · 1-2 days
  • First
  • 25% · 7 days
  • Second
  • 50% · 14 days
  • Broad
  • 28 days
  • Prerequisites
  • Windows 10 21H2 or 11 Pro / Enterprise / Education
  • Device-based Intune enrollment
  • Intune Premium (or M365 E3 / E5)
  • Microsoft 365 Apps installed
  • Enable & track
  • Enable in Tenant administration > Windows Autopatch
  • Pilot with IT devices for 4-6 weeks
  • Track the dashboard for ring health, paused updates, compliance : target 95%+
  • A device can't be in both an Autopatch ring and a separate update ring for the same update type.
Slide 39

Troubleshoot update deployment and compliance issues

  • Diagnose
  • Read compliance & ring reports
  • Pause
  • Pause a ring if failures > 5%
  • Fix
  • Remediate app, driver, or disk
  • Common causes
  • Storage : devices need 10-15 GB free; nearly full disks fail mid-install
  • Connectivity : dropped downloads corrupt feature updates
  • Apps & drivers : API changes break old apps; outdated drivers block updates
  • BIOS / firmware : older BIOS missing VBS or UEFI causes failures
  • Clustered failures : same error across many devices = a shared cause
  • Device-side diagnostics
  • Get-WindowsUpdateLog
  • SetupDiag : setup root-cause analysis
  • CBS & DISM component staging logs
Slide 40

Retire and reset devices using Microsoft Intune

  • Retire
  • Unenrolls from Intune; data stays
  • User keeps files and apps
  • Use for offboarding or BYOD exit
  • Stays in reports 90 days, then removed
  • Wipe
  • Erases company data and apps
  • Keeps personal files intact
  • Use for reassigning a device
  • Re-enrollment is immediate
  • Reset
  • Factory reset : like new
  • Removes all user data and apps
  • Boots into Windows Setup
  • Re-enroll via Autopilot or manual
  • Operate at scale
  • Back up data & warn users first
  • Stolen devices: wipe, not retire
  • Bulk device action for fleets
  • Log ID, owner, date for audit

Oorspronkelijke kennisvragen uit de cursus (Engels)

Unchanged question wording from the source decks, apart from typography. Answers and explanations, where present, come from the associated speaker notes. Treat them as course-source answers; validate current product behavior in Microsoft documentation.

Slide 41 · Question 1

Contoso has 10,000 Windows devices. Finance devices run legacy accounting software that requires extensive testing before updates. Marketing benefits from rapid feature access. Executives want immediate security patches but no feature updates. Which update ring configuration best addresses these requirements?

  1. All devices in a single ring with 60-day deferral
  2. Finance: 90-day feature deferral; Marketing: 30-day deferral; Executives: 7-day security deferral with Hotpatch enabled, feature updates deferred
  3. Use Autopatch for all devices with a uniform 30-day deferral
Answer and source explanation

Q1 - Correct: B. Finance: 90-day feature deferral; Marketing: 30-day deferral; Executives: 7-day security deferral with Hotpatch enabled, feature updates deferred Correct. This configuration addresses each group's specific needs: Finance gets lengthy deferral for legacy software compatibility, Marketing gets faster updates for feature adoption, and Executives get rapid security patching via Hotpatch while feature updates are deferred. A. Incorrect. A single ring with one deferral period cannot address the distinct requirements of Finance (long testing), Marketing (rapid features), and Executives (fast security, deferred features). You need separate rings with tailored deferral settings. C. Incorrect. A uniform 30-day deferral doesn't address Finance's need for extended testing, Marketing's need for faster access, or Executives' need for rapid security patches with deferred features.

Slide 41 · Question 2

You want to roll out Hotpatch to 500 devices in the Operations department. All devices run Windows 11 Enterprise, have Secure Boot enabled, and use processors from 2018 or newer. However, 50 devices have virtualization-based security (VBS) disabled. How many devices are eligible for Hotpatch?

  1. 500 devices
  2. 450 devices
  3. 0 devices
Answer and source explanation

Q2 - Correct: B. 450 devices Correct. Hotpatch requires Windows 11 Enterprise (met), a modern processor (met for all 500), Secure Boot enabled (met), and VBS enabled. The 50 devices with VBS disabled do not meet requirements, leaving 450 eligible devices. A. Incorrect. Not all 500 devices qualify. Hotpatch requires VBS to be enabled, and 50 devices have VBS disabled, making them ineligible. C. Incorrect. Most devices meet the Hotpatch prerequisites. Only the 50 devices with VBS disabled are ineligible : the remaining 450 qualify.

Slide 41 · Question 3

You deployed a feature update to your pilot ring of 200 devices. 180 succeeded, but 20 show error code 0x8024401C. Users on the failing devices report less than 2 GB of free disk space. What is the most likely cause and best resolution?

  1. Network connectivity : redeploy the update during a different time window
  2. Insufficient storage : guide users to free disk space to at least 10 GB
  3. BIOS or firmware incompatibility : schedule BIOS updates for the affected devices
Answer and source explanation

Q3 - Correct: B. Insufficient storage : guide users to free disk space to at least 10 GB Correct. Feature updates require 10-15 GB of free disk space, and 2 GB is far below that threshold. Freeing sufficient space resolves the issue for the affected devices. A. Incorrect. Network connectivity issues would affect devices more broadly and intermittently. The consistent pattern of the same 20 devices failing with low disk space points to a storage issue rather than a network issue. C. Incorrect. BIOS incompatibility issues typically produce different error codes and wouldn't consistently align with devices that have low disk space. The storage shortage is the clear root cause here.

Slide 42 · Question 1

Your organization has three device groups: manufacturing control systems (uptime critical), standard business laptops, and a research department that needs early access to new Windows features. Which servicing channel assignment is most appropriate?

  1. Manufacturing: LTSC; Standard: Current Channel; Research: Windows Insider (Preview) Channel
  2. All devices on LTSC for organization-wide stability
  3. Manufacturing: LTSC; Standard and Research: Current Channel
Answer and source explanation

Q4 - Correct: A. Manufacturing: LTSC; Standard: Current Channel; Research: Windows Insider (Preview) Channel Correct. LTSC gives manufacturing systems long-term stability with infrequent feature updates. Current Channel suits standard devices with regular feature releases. The Preview Channel gives the research team early access to new Windows features and APIs. B. Incorrect. LTSC is intended for devices that require minimal change, not general business use. Using LTSC for standard and research devices would prevent regular feature updates and deny the research team access to cutting-edge Windows capabilities. C. Incorrect. While manufacturing correctly uses LTSC, assigning the research department to Current Channel denies them early access to preview features. The research team's requirement specifically calls for the Preview Channel.

Slide 42 · Question 2

A user leaves your organization and returns their company laptop, which contained financial data and was used on public networks. You need to redeploy the device to a new employee immediately. Which device lifecycle action should you use?

  1. Retire
  2. Wipe
  3. Reset
Answer and source explanation

Q5 - Correct: C. Reset Correct. Reset removes all data including financial information, returns the device to a factory state, and prepares it for fresh enrollment and configuration. This is the most secure and appropriate lifecycle action for redeploying a device to a new employee. A. Incorrect. Retire removes company data and configuration but leaves personal data intact and doesn't fully prepare the device for a new user. It's intended for devices being decommissioned, not for redeployment. B. Incorrect. Wipe removes company-managed data but retains some personal data depending on configuration. For a complete, secure reset that fully prepares the device for a new employee, Reset is the appropriate action.

Learning Path 02Module 04

Apparaat- en beleidsproblemen oplossen

Je volgt een vaste diagnosevolgorde van tenantgezondheid tot clientlogs en gerichte correctie.

← Vorige moduleVolgende module →↑ Terug naar boven

Waarom is deze module belangrijk?

Een gestructureerde aanpak verkleint de kans op onnodig opnieuw inschrijven of het verzwakken van beveiligingsbeleid.

Leerdoelen

  • Uitleggen: scope en servicegezondheid.
  • Uitleggen: gebruikersgerichte diagnose.
  • Uitleggen: beleidsstatus en clientlogs.
  • Uitleggen: herstel en escalatie.

01. Scope en servicegezondheid

Noteer UPN, Device ID, tijdstip, foutcode en verwacht gedrag. Bepaal of één gebruiker, één model of de hele tenant geraakt is. Controleer service health en recente wijzigingen. Bij een brede storing voorkom je ongecoördineerde lokale aanpassingen en communiceer je de bekende impact.

02. Gebruikersgerichte diagnose

De Intune-weergave Troubleshooting + support brengt licentie, groepen, apparaten en relevante status bij elkaar. Onderzoek inschrijving afzonderlijk van compliance. Vergelijk de gebruiker en het apparaat met een werkend voorbeeld. Controleer daarnaast Entra-aanmeldlogs om vast te stellen waarom toegang geweigerd wordt.

03. Beleidsstatus en clientlogs

Lees de instelling die werkelijk faalt en controleer alle toewijzingen. Gebruik Windows MDM-diagnostiek en DeviceManagement-Enterprise-Diagnostics-Provider-events voor MDM-beleid. Voor Win32-apps zijn Intune Management Extension-logs relevant. Leg de foutcode en context vast; een algemene status zonder details is onvoldoende voor oorzaakanalyse.

04. Herstel en escalatie

Corrigeer de kleinste aantoonbare oorzaak, synchroniseer en controleer opnieuw. Automatiseer terugkerende, goed begrepen problemen met geteste Remediations. Verzamel bij escalatie tijdstippen, correlatiegegevens en diagnostiek. Bewaar geen secrets in het supportdossier en documenteer ook welke oplossingen niet geholpen hebben.

Wat moet je als beheerder onthouden?

  • Bepaal eerst de omvang en controleer service health.
  • Gebruik logs van de juiste beheercomponent.
  • Verifieer het herstel vanuit het perspectief van de gebruiker.

Samenvatting

Bepaal eerst de omvang en controleer service health. Gebruik logs van de juiste beheercomponent. Verifieer het herstel vanuit het perspectief van de gebruiker. Gebruik de hoofdstukken om keuzes te onderbouwen en controleer altijd de effectieve toestand naast de portalstatus.

Cursusverdieping per onderwerp (Engels) · 11 bronslides

Original course content. Text order follows the source presentation; visual tables and diagrams are represented as text.

Slide 45

Understand the troubleshooting workflow in Microsoft Intune

  • Work top-down: scope the problem, rule out service-wide issues, then drill in.
  • Phase 1 · Scope the issue
  • Capture UPN and Device ID
  • Note expected vs actual behavior
  • Determine reach: one user, model, site, or tenant
  • One user = local / group · everyone = service / global
  • Phase 2 · Check service health
  • Tenant administration > Tenant status
  • Look for Service Incidents or Advisories
  • If an incident is open, stop and wait
  • Phase 3 · Run the portal
  • Troubleshooting + support > Troubleshoot
  • Select the affected user
  • Review license, groups, device status, enrollment failures
Slide 46

Troubleshooting workflow : status to remediation

  • Phase 4 · Inspect policy & app
  • Devices > All devices → the failing item
  • Read the exact error code
  • A Conflict = two policies fighting : resolve the overlap first
  • Phase 5 · Pull client logs
  • Force a Sync, then collect logs
  • Windows: Collect diagnostics or Event Viewer
  • iOS / Android: Company Portal → Send logs
  • Phase 6 · Remediate or escalate
  • Fix the conflict, adjust group membership, or remove the stale record
  • OS-level failure or service bug → open a ticket via Help and support with UPN, Device ID, error codes
  • With Copilot
  • Triage faster with prompts like “Why is this device noncompliant?” or “Compare this device to Desktop01.” Dedicated agents handle change review, device offboarding, policy config, and vulnerability remediation.
Slide 47

Troubleshoot device enrollment and compliance failures

  • Enrollment brings a device under management. Compliance keeps it on the baseline. A failure in either gate blocks Conditional Access : and the user.
  • Gate 1 · Device enrollment failures
  • Symptoms: version not supported, personal devices not allowed, or max devices reached
  • Error codes 80180014 / 80180003 during MDM enrollment
  • Setup completes but the device never appears in Intune
  • 1 Verify licensing & MDM scope
  • Confirm a valid Intune license; check the MDM user scope covers the user
  • 2 Check Enrollment Restrictions
  • Most common cause : platform (OS + ownership) and device-limit restrictions
  • 3 Review Autopilot / ADE
  • Confirm the hardware hash / serial is assigned to a synced profile
Slide 48

Troubleshoot device compliance failures

  • Gate 2 · Compliance failures
  • Suddenly blocked from Teams, Outlook, or M365
  • Status shows Not compliant or In grace period
  • Not evaluated : policy never landed or doesn't apply
  • 1 Identify the failing setting
  • Devices → All devices → Device compliance reveals the exact setting
  • 2 Check device sync status
  • Compliant locally but stale in the cloud : trigger Sync
  • 3 Review policy assignment
  • Unassigned devices count as Compliant by default : flip this setting
  • 4 Investigate 'Not evaluated'
  • Policy hasn't reached the device : check check-in time and OS scope
  • Best practices: 3-day grace for minor infractions · auto-email fix guidance · cross-reference Conditional Access sign-in logs.
Slide 49

Troubleshoot configuration profile and policy conflicts

  • Policy A
  • Disable camera · assigned to All Devices
  • CONFLICT
  • Same setting, different values
  • Policy B
  • Enable camera · assigned to Marketing
  • When policies overlap
  • Result
  • Compliance · Configuration
  • Compliance wins
  • Configuration · Configuration
  • Conflict
  • Endpoint security · Device config
  • Conflict
  • Custom OMA-URI · Standard UI
  • UI wins
Slide 50

Spot and fix policy conflicts

  • Spot it
  • Profile view → Status column
  • Device view → Device configuration
  • A Conflict status flags the overlapping settings
  • Fix it
  • Troubleshoot portal → check the user's Groups
  • Add exclusion groups on the broader policy
  • Consolidate overlapping settings via the Settings Catalog
  • MAM exception
  • App Protection Policies never conflict : Intune applies the most restrictive value (e.g. a 6-digit PIN over a 4-digit PIN).
Slide 51

Use logs and diagnostics to investigate device issues

  • Remote pull · via Intune
  • Devices > All devices → pick the failing Windows device → Collect diagnostics
  • Monitor > Device diagnostics → row menu → Download
  • Open MDMDiagReport.html for applied policies; .evtx files for raw events
  • With Copilot: pick a device → Summarize with Copilot for a single pane
  • Local collection · on the device
  • Windows : Advanced Diagnostic Report: Settings > Accounts > Access work or school > Info > Create report
  • Output: C:\Users\Public\Documents\MDMDiagnostics\
  • iOS / Android : Company Portal → Send logs (shake on iOS) → returns an Incident ID
Slide 52

Read MDM events and fleet-wide signals

  • Event Viewer → Applications and Services Logs → Microsoft → Windows → DeviceManagement-Enterprise-Diagnostics-Provider → Admin
  • Event ID
  • Meaning
  • 814
  • Policy applied successfully : setting reached the local registry
  • 404
  • Policy failed : description shows the failing CSP / registry path
  • 11
  • Sync session started with the Intune management server
  • Proactive telemetry · Endpoint analytics
  • Startup performance : boot time + what slows it
  • Application reliability : crash rate per app
  • Work from anywhere : cloud identity & management readiness
  • Advanced Analytics : deeper reports (separate Intune Suite license)
Slide 53

Use the Intune Troubleshooting blade for user-based diagnostics

  • Helpdesk ticket · Alex: “I enrolled my new iPad, but I can't open corporate email : it says my device is blocked.”
  • 1 Search the user
  • Troubleshooting + support > Troubleshoot → select Alex. Enabled · License Yes · Group Mobile Users : not an identity issue
  • 2 Open the Devices tab
  • Laptop + iPad listed. Windows laptop Compliant; iPad Non-compliant : Conditional Access is blocking email
  • 3 Drill into the iPad
  • Device Compliance → expand the failing policy. Root cause: baseline requires iOS 16.0; device on iOS 15.4
  • 4 Reply to the ticket
  • No Intune changes needed : send a self-serve fix
  • Self-serve remediation
  • Alex: open Settings → General → Software Update, install the latest iOS, then sync Company Portal : email unlocks. Resolved in under two minutes without changing any policy.
Slide 54

Automate issue resolution using remediation scripts

  • 1 · Detection
  • One check, on schedule
  • 2 · Remediation
  • The fix : silent
  • Detection script
  • Runs on a schedule to check device state
  • Exit 0 : healthy, done
  • Exit 1 : issue found, fire the fix
  • Remediation use cases
  • Restart stopped services
  • Clear stale caches
  • Enforce registry keys
  • Fix broken shortcuts
Slide 55

Deploy and monitor remediations

  • Deploy from Devices > Manage devices > Scripts and remediations
  • 1 Basics
  • Name the package
  • 2 Settings
  • Upload both .ps1 files : detection + remediation
  • 3 Assignments
  • Entra group + schedule
  • Monitor results
  • Without issues · Issue fixed · Failed devices
  • Shortcuts
  • Built-in : assign ready-made packages, skip scripting
  • On-demand : run remediation on one device for a live ticket

1. Na een tenantbrede foutmelding raken veel gebruikers tegelijk toegang kwijt. Waar begin je?

2. Welk logtype hoort bij Win32-appdistributie?

Oorspronkelijke kennisvragen uit de cursus (Engels)

Unchanged question wording from the source decks, apart from typography. Answers and explanations, where present, come from the associated speaker notes. Treat them as course-source answers; validate current product behavior in Microsoft documentation.

Slide 56 · Question 1

A user completes Windows Autopilot setup but their device shows 'Not enrolled' in the Intune admin center 30 minutes later. The user successfully signed in with their Microsoft Entra account. What should you check first?

  1. Whether an Intune license is assigned to the user's account
  2. Whether Secure Boot is enabled on the device
  3. Whether the device's MDM enrollment URL is correctly configured in DNS
Answer and source explanation

Q1 - Correct: A. Whether an Intune license is assigned to the user's account Correct. Microsoft Entra ID authentication can succeed even when the user has no Intune license. Without a license, enrollment finishes the sign-in but fails to register the device in Intune. B. Incorrect. Secure Boot affects compliance policy evaluation but isn't a prerequisite for enrollment. A device can enroll in Intune without Secure Boot enabled: it would simply be marked noncompliant if a policy requires it. C. Incorrect. Autopilot uses preconfigured service discovery and doesn't rely on DNS-based MDM enrollment URLs. A failed license assignment is a far more common cause of enrollment not appearing in the admin center.

Slide 56 · Question 2

A Windows device enrolled in Intune shows a 'Conflict' status for a VPN configuration profile. The device is online and checking in. What is the most likely cause?

  1. The profile platform is incompatible with the device's Windows edition
  2. Two or more configuration profiles assigned to the device configure the same VPN setting with different values
  3. The device hasn't synced with Intune since the profile was last modified
Answer and source explanation

Q2 - Correct: B. Two or more configuration profiles assigned to the device configure the same VPN setting with different values Correct. A Conflict status means multiple applicable profiles are attempting to set the same setting to different values and Intune can't determine which takes precedence. Review all profiles assigned to the affected device groups and remove or consolidate the conflicting settings. A. Incorrect. Platform incompatibility would cause the profile to show as 'Not applicable' for the device rather than 'Conflict.' Conflict specifically means the device received multiple policies that configure the same setting with contradictory values. C. Incorrect. A device that hasn't synced would show a 'Pending' status, not 'Conflict.' Conflict is reported after the device has received and evaluated the assigned policies.

Slide 56 · Question 3

A user calls the help desk saying they can't access company email on their personal device. Intune shows the device as noncompliant. What is the most likely reason they're being blocked?

  1. The user's Intune license has expired
  2. A Conditional Access policy is requiring device compliance as a condition for accessing Exchange Online
  3. The device's enrollment token has expired and needs to be re-enrolled
Answer and source explanation

Q3 - Correct: B. A Conditional Access policy is requiring device compliance as a condition for accessing Exchange Online Correct. When a Conditional Access policy requires compliant devices for accessing Exchange Online, any device marked noncompliant in Intune is blocked from that resource. The user needs to resolve the noncompliance issue (such as setting a PIN or enabling encryption) to regain access. A. Incorrect. An expired license would prevent device management and check-ins, but it's not the mechanism that blocks resource access. The described scenario: noncompliant device blocked from email: is a Conditional Access enforcement pattern. C. Incorrect. MDM enrollment tokens don't work this way for personal device scenarios. The device shows as enrolled and noncompliant: the access block is from Conditional Access evaluating the compliance state, not an enrollment issue.

Slide 57 · Question 1

You need to investigate why a specific user's device isn't receiving an assigned app. Which tool in the Intune admin center gives you a single view of that user's device assignments, policy evaluations, and app deployment status?

  1. Endpoint Analytics > Device scores
  2. The Intune Troubleshooting blade (Troubleshooting + support > Troubleshoot)
  3. Devices > Monitor > Device compliance
Answer and source explanation

Q4 - Correct: B. The Intune Troubleshooting blade (Troubleshooting + support > Troubleshoot) Correct. The Troubleshooting blade lets you search for a specific user and see all their enrolled devices, assigned policies, app assignments, and evaluation results in one place. It's the fastest way to identify why a specific user or device isn't receiving an expected assignment. A. Incorrect. Endpoint Analytics provides performance and health metrics across the device fleet. It doesn't show per-user policy evaluation results or app assignment details for troubleshooting a specific user's issue. C. Incorrect. The Device compliance monitor shows compliance state across devices but doesn't provide per-user app assignment details or policy evaluation results. The Troubleshooting blade is purpose-built for this type of user-specific investigation.

Slide 57 · Question 2

After a software deployment, the Windows Time service keeps getting disabled on devices, which breaks Kerberos authentication. The issue recurs every few days. What is the best way to address it?

  1. Create a compliance policy that marks devices noncompliant when the Windows Time service is disabled
  2. Deploy a Remediation script package that detects when the Windows Time service is disabled and automatically restarts and re-enables it
  3. Assign a device restriction configuration profile that prevents users from modifying Windows services
Answer and source explanation

Q5 - Correct: B. Deploy a Remediation script package that detects when the Windows Time service is disabled and automatically restarts and re-enables it Correct. A Remediation script package pairs a detection script that checks device state with a remediation script that fixes the issue. On a scheduled cadence, the package restores the Windows Time service whenever it's disabled, with no manual intervention. A. Incorrect. A compliance policy would flag the affected devices as noncompliant and potentially block access, but it doesn't fix the underlying issue. The service would remain disabled until an admin manually re-enables it on each device. C. Incorrect. Device restriction profiles can limit user actions but aren't well-suited to monitoring and correcting specific service states after the fact. A Remediation script is the right tool for detecting and fixing a recurring configuration drift issue.
