Learning Path 03

Applicaties beheren

5 modules · Nederlands met Engelstalige cursusverdieping

Learning Path 03Module 01

Applicaties uitrollen en beheren

Je kiest appsoorten, installatiecontext, detectieregels en toewijzingen voor betrouwbare softwaredistributie.

← Vorige moduleVolgende module →↑ Terug naar boven

Waarom is deze module belangrijk?

Een correct pakket kan toch falen door onjuiste context, afhankelijkheden of detectie. Die onderdelen horen bij het appontwerp.

Leerdoelen

  • Uitleggen: de juiste appsoort.
  • Uitleggen: doelgroep en installatiegedrag.
  • Uitleggen: requirements, dependencies en detection.
  • Uitleggen: onderhoud en foutanalyse.

01. De juiste appsoort

Vergelijk Microsoft Store, Microsoft 365 Apps, Win32 en line-of-business-distributie. Een Win32-app gebruikt doorgaans een .intunewin-pakket en expliciete installatie- en detectielogica. Store- en Microsoft 365-distributie hebben hun eigen beheermodel. Kies op basis van installatiebron, platform, onderhoud en vereiste controle.

02. Doelgroep en installatiegedrag

Required installeert verplicht; Available biedt installatie via Company Portal in ondersteunde scenario’s; Uninstall verwijdert via de toegewezen intentie. Kies gebruikers- of systeemcontext volgens de installer. Een gebruikersinterface in een stille systeeminstallatie kan de uitrol blokkeren. Test alle installatieparameters op een representatieve client.

03. Requirements, dependencies en detection

Requirements bepalen geschiktheid. Dependencies verzorgen vereiste Win32-afhankelijkheden. Detection bepaalt of de gewenste app aanwezig is. Een installer die eindigt met succes maar een onjuiste detectieregel heeft, kan toch als mislukt verschijnen. Gebruik een regel die versie en installatielocatie correct weerspiegelt.

04. Onderhoud en foutanalyse

Plan updatekanalen en opvolgende versies vooraf. Gebruik app install status, foutcodes en logs zoals AppWorkload.log en IntuneManagementExtension.log waar van toepassing. Controleer download, uitvoeringscontext, retourcode en detectie afzonderlijk. Een succesvolle installatie is het begin van beheer, niet het einde van de lifecycle.

Wat moet je als beheerder onthouden?

  • Requirements, installatie en detectie hebben elk een eigen functie.
  • Test stille installatie en uninstall in de juiste context.
  • Required, Available en Uninstall drukken verschillende intenties uit.

Samenvatting

Requirements, installatie en detectie hebben elk een eigen functie. Test stille installatie en uninstall in de juiste context. Required, Available en Uninstall drukken verschillende intenties uit. Gebruik de hoofdstukken om keuzes te onderbouwen en controleer altijd de effectieve toestand naast de portalstatus.

Cursusverdieping per onderwerp (Engels) · 13 bronslides

Original course content. Text order follows the source presentation; visual tables and diagrams are represented as text.

Slide 5

Understand application deployment options in Microsoft Intune

  • Three deployment decisions
  • App type · assignment intent · targeting
  • 1 · App type
  • Pick the package method that fits the software and control you need
  • Win32 : .intunewin + detection rules
  • Microsoft Store : catalog, auto updates
  • Microsoft 365 : Click-to-Run, channel-based
  • Windows Package Manager : WinGet, no packaging
  • 2 · Assignment intent
  • Decide how the app reaches devices and how much user choice
  • Required : auto-installs and stays
  • Available : optional via Company Portal
  • Uninstall : removed on next device sync
  • Context: System (all users) or user
  • 3 · Targeting
  • Pick who receives the app and the context it installs into
  • Entra ID groups : security or dynamic
  • Dynamic filters : OS, manufacturer, compliance
  • Inclusions / exclusions : no new groups
  • Monitoring : installs, errors, devices to check
Slide 6

Application deployment options : app type and assignment reference

  • App types at a glance
  • App type
  • Package
  • Notes
  • Win32
  • .intunewin
  • Detection rules confirm install
  • Microsoft Store
  • Store catalog
  • Automatic updates via WinGet
  • Microsoft 365
  • Click-to-Run
  • Channel-based servicing
  • Windows Package Manager
  • WinGet catalog
  • No packaging required
  • Assignment & targeting
  • Assignment intents
  • Required : auto-install, kept installed, retried on failure
  • Available : user installs on demand from Company Portal
  • Uninstall : removed from targeted devices on next sync
  • Targeting controls
  • Entra ID groups : assigned or dynamic membership
  • Assignment filters : deviceOSType, ownership, compliance
  • Include / exclude : refine reach without new groups
Slide 7

Plan application deployment for your organization

  • Plan before you deploy : four steps from discovery to rollout
  • 1 · Discover apps
  • Inventory apps in use : versions, owners, groups
  • Categorize : critical, supporting, legacy
  • Owners : a per-app contact for issues
  • 2 · Map dependencies
  • Runtimes : .NET, Visual C++, Java
  • OS & hardware : min OS, GPU, drivers
  • Special : admin rights, network, licensing
  • 3 · Pick method
  • Installer : MSI, EXE, APPX, web
  • Intune option : Win32, Store, M365, LOB
  • Repackage? : customize, bundle, strip
  • 4 · Test & roll out
  • Pilot group : mixed devices, sites, networks
  • User acceptance : UAT for critical apps
  • Phased : pilot → departments → org
  • Timing, bandwidth & targeting
  • Schedule large packages off-hours, stagger group rollouts to spread network load, and test updates in a pilot first. Target with department / role groups and dynamic filters so users get what they need without clutter.
Slide 8

Deploy Microsoft Store apps using Microsoft Intune

  • Free Store apps · direct from catalog
  • Microsoft Store app (new) : Intune searches the catalog and installs via WinGet + the Intune Management Extension
  • Add from the admin center · pick User or System context · assign Required / Available / Uninstall
  • Paid or commercial · another path
  • Store for Business retired : acquire and license outside Store integration
  • Enterprise App Catalog when the app exists · Win32 packaging for installers you control · publisher volume licensing
  • Area
  • Free Store app
  • Paid or commercial
  • App source
  • Microsoft Store catalog via WinGet
  • Enterprise App Catalog or publisher
  • Install method
  • IME + WinGet client
  • MSI, EXE, or vendor installer
  • Install context
  • User or System (per app)
  • Defined by the Win32 package
  • Updates
  • Automatic · no Intune scheduling
  • Managed through your Win32 update flow
  • Licensing
  • Free
  • Volume licensing outside the Store
  • Confirm network paths : Microsoft Store, WinGet, and content endpoints must be reachable; the Intune Management Extension is required on every target device.
Slide 9

Deploy Microsoft 365 apps using Microsoft Intune

  • Build the suite once, assign by intent, then monitor adoption and failures
  • 1 · Configure
  • Pick apps and channel : build the suite once
  • Apps : Word, Excel, Outlook, PowerPoint; only what each group needs
  • Architecture : 64-bit by default; 32-bit only for legacy fit
  • Update channel : Current · Monthly Enterprise · Semi-Annual Enterprise
  • 2 · Assign & deploy
  • Right intent, right audience : before installs begin
  • Intent : Required, Available, or Uninstall
  • Targeting : Entra ID groups plus dynamic filters (e.g. “Windows 10, 4 GB RAM or more”)
  • Existing Office : coexist with older versions or remove during install
  • 3 · Monitor
  • Apps > All apps : start here for every issue
  • Install status : successes, pending, failures per device
  • Common causes : disk space, OS version, network reachability
  • Adoption : track active use; switch channels if needs change
Slide 10

Deploy Win32 apps using Microsoft Intune

  • 1 · Package
  • Build the .intunewin with the Content Prep Tool
  • 2 · On device
  • Detection + requirements confirm the install
  • Package inputs
  • Source folder : installer + supporting files
  • Setup file : setup.exe or application.msi
  • Command line : silent switch /S or /quiet
  • Detection rules : how Intune confirms success
  • File : path and version under Program Files
  • Registry : key the installer creates on success
  • MSI : MSI product code; most reliable for MSI
  • Requirement rules : who the app applies to
  • Architecture : x64, x86, or ARM64; non-matching show as Not applicable
  • OS & disk : minimum Windows version and free space
Slide 11

Deploy Win32 apps : the three levers that shape the rollout

  • Return codes
  • Match the installer's exit codes
  • 0 means success
  • 3010 marks a soft failure : the device restarts later without retrying
  • Dependencies
  • Prerequisites install first
  • Add .NET or any required runtime as a dependency : Intune installs it before the app
  • Up to 100 dependencies (including nested)
  • Supersedence
  • Replace older versions cleanly
  • Mark the previous version superseded : Intune uninstalls it and installs the new one
  • Chains up to 10 versions (including the app)
Slide 12

Deploy line-of-business (LOB) apps with Intune

  • LOB apps live only inside your organization. Package once, upload to Intune, and let centralized assignment, updates, and reporting carry the rest.
  • 0 · Plan
  • Format : .msi, .msix, .ipa, .apk, .pkg
  • Architecture : x86, x64, or ARM64
  • Dependencies : frameworks the installer needs
  • Audience : users, devices, rollout phases
  • 1 · Package
  • Pick the format for the platform
  • Windows : MSI, MSIX, APPX, APPXBundle
  • Apple : IPA (iOS/iPadOS), PKG (macOS)
  • Android : APK or managed Google Play
  • 2 · Upload
  • Apps > All apps > Create : pick platform, upload package
  • Metadata : name, publisher, version, category
  • Settings : install args, uninstall, detection rules
  • MSIX auto-detects dependencies and requirements
  • 3 · Deploy & update
  • Assignments : Required, Available, or Uninstall · groups + filters
  • Updates : upload a new version; MSIX swaps in the background
  • Monitor : successes, pending, failures per device
Slide 13

Configure app availability, targeting, and install behavior

  • Intent · how users receive it
  • Required installs automatically and stays; Available appears in Company Portal so users choose; Uninstall removes the app from devices that already have it
  • Audience · who receives it
  • Target Entra ID user or device groups, lean on dynamic groups for self-maintenance, then narrow with assignment filters on OS, ownership, and model
  • Assignment behavior at a glance
  • Behavior
  • Required
  • Available
  • Install moment
  • Automatic on next check-in
  • When the user picks it from Company Portal
  • User control
  • Cannot uninstall the app
  • User chooses to install or remove
  • Best fit
  • Security tools and core productivity
  • Role-specific or optional utilities
  • Retry on failure
  • Intune retries automatically
  • User can retry from Company Portal
  • Context
  • User or device context per app
  • Often user context · per profile
  • Not applicable
  • Device does not meet requirements
  • Usually OS version or hardware mismatch
Slide 14

Configure app update and assignment settings

  • 1 · App update model
  • Store apps : auto-update via Microsoft Store
  • Win32 : versioned packages controlled in Intune
  • LOB & MSIX : upload a new package to update
  • Microsoft 365 : Current, Monthly, Semi-Annual
  • 2 · Assignment intent
  • Required : auto-install, re-attempt until done
  • Available : optional in Company Portal
  • Uninstall : removes the app from targets
  • Tip: Required keeps versions uniform
  • 3 · Targeting & groups
  • User groups : apps follow the user across devices
  • Device groups : apps stick to specific machines
  • Filters : refine on OS, model, ownership
  • 4 · Monitor & verify
  • Installed : confirmed on the device
  • Pending : awaiting check-in or retry
  • Failed : error during install
  • Not applicable : requirements not met
  • Match control to the app's risk profile
  • Store apps update automatically : convenient but not schedulable. Win32 and LOB give granular version control when compliance matters. Assign as Required and the next version installs on check-in : run a pilot first, then expand. Available leaves the version choice to the user.
Slide 15

Monitor app deployment and troubleshoot failures

  • 1 · Portal view
  • Quick health check : open the app pane in the Intune admin center
  • Installed : confirm detection matches
  • Failed / pending : open device or user status, grab the error code
  • Not applicable : OS, architecture, filter, or requirement mismatch
  • 2 · User & device view
  • Use Troubleshoot + support for the named user or device
  • Assignment reaches the device? : targeting, exclusion, filter
  • App applies? : requirements, OS version, architecture
  • Install starts? : installer execution and content handling
  • 3 · Client logs
  • Collect diagnostics from the device pane
  • Read the client logs from the IntuneManagementExtension\Logs folder
  • Confirm the exact failure point when the portal isn't enough
Slide 16

Monitor and troubleshoot : Intune Management Extension client logs

  • On Windows, the IME writes detailed client logs under ProgramData. Open these when the portal doesn't explain the failure.
  • Log file
  • What it records
  • IntuneManagementExtension.log
  • Agent check-in, policy request, and processing
  • AppWorkload.log
  • Install attempts, applicability, and detection
  • AppActionProcessor.log
  • Detection and applicability checks for app actions
  • AgentExecutor.log
  • PowerShell script execution details
Slide 17

Manage app deployment failures with logs and tools

  • Admin center · start here
  • Troubleshoot + support : investigate a single user and device end-to-end first
  • Search by name or email → open Summary · Applications
  • User install status shows the failure detail before you open any log
  • Client logs · on the device
  • Path : ProgramData \ IntuneManagementExtension \ Logs
  • Collect remotely : Device details · Collect diagnostics
  • Open AppWorkload.log, IntuneManagementExtension.log, AgentExecutor.log
  • Three failure patterns : and how to clear them
  • Error
  • Symptom
  • How to clear it
  • 0x87D1041C
  • App installed but no longer detected
  • User may have removed it : Required apps reinstall on the next sync
  • 0x80073CFF
  • Sideloading not enabled
  • Confirm the device is domain-joined or has AllowAllTrustedApps enabled
  • Detection mismatch
  • Installed but reported failed
  • Test the detection rule on a working device : wrong path or key is the usual culprit

Kenniscontrole

Kies per vraag één antwoord. Je kunt opnieuw proberen; de score bepaalt je modulevoortgang niet.

1. De installatie slaagt, maar Intune rapporteert dat de app niet gevonden is. Wat controleer je?

2. Een app moet optioneel beschikbaar zijn via Company Portal. Welke intentie past?

Oorspronkelijke kennisvragen uit de cursus (Engels)

Unchanged question wording from the source decks, apart from typography. Answers and explanations, where present, come from the associated speaker notes. Treat them as course-source answers; validate current product behavior in Microsoft documentation.

Slide 18 · Question 1

You're the endpoint administrator at Contoso. Sales wants a new expense-reporting Win32 app installed automatically on every sales laptop, but the marketing team should be able to install it only if they choose to. What assignment configuration meets both requirements?

  1. Create one Required assignment targeting both the Sales and Marketing groups.
  2. Create a Required assignment for the Sales group and an Available for enrolled devices assignment for the Marketing group.
  3. Create a Required assignment for Sales and an Uninstall assignment for Marketing.
Answer and source explanation

Q1 - Correct: B. Create a Required assignment for the Sales group and an Available for enrolled devices assignment for the Marketing group. Correct. Required forces the install for Sales, while Available makes the same app visible in the Company Portal so Marketing users can install it on demand. A. A Required assignment installs the app automatically for everyone in the targeted groups, which doesn't give Marketing the option to skip it. C. Uninstall would actively remove the app from Marketing devices. Marketing users should be able to install it themselves, not have it removed.

Slide 18 · Question 2

You're packaging a custom Win32 line-of-business app for Intune deployment. Which file format must you produce before you can upload the app to the Intune admin center?

  1. .appx
  2. .intunewin
  3. .msi
Answer and source explanation

Q2 - Correct: B. .intunewin Correct. The Microsoft Win32 Content Prep Tool wraps your installer and supporting files into an .intunewin package, which is the format Intune requires for Win32 app deployments. A. .appx is the format for modern Windows Store apps, not Win32 packages prepared for Intune. C. .msi is a standard Windows installer format, but Intune doesn't deploy raw .msi files for Win32 apps. You must first wrap the .msi in an .intunewin package.

Slide 18 · Question 3

You deployed a Win32 finance application as Required to 200 devices. The Intune deployment report shows 180 devices as Installed and 20 as Failed. You open the Microsoft Intune admin center to investigate one of the failed devices. Where do you go first to get device-specific diagnostic detail without having to remote into the machine?

  1. Open the Microsoft Defender portal and review the device timeline.
  2. Open the Troubleshoot + support pane, search for the user, select the device, and review the Applications section.
  3. Open Microsoft Entra ID and review the device sign-in logs.
Answer and source explanation

Q3 - Correct: B. Open the Troubleshoot + support pane, search for the user, select the device, and review the Applications section. Correct. The Troubleshoot + support pane in the Intune admin center lets you drill into a specific user and device, identify failed apps, and trigger a sync or collect diagnostics : all without local device access. A. The Microsoft Defender portal surfaces security events, not Intune app installation diagnostics. C. Microsoft Entra sign-in logs show authentication events, not app installation results.

Slide 19 · Question 1

An app reports Installed on the device, but Intune still shows the install as Failed in the admin center. What's the most likely cause?

  1. The user uninstalled the app shortly after installation completed.
  2. The detection rule doesn't match what the installer actually placed on the device.
  3. The device is offline and hasn't checked in with Intune.
Answer and source explanation

Q4 - Correct: B. The detection rule doesn't match what the installer actually placed on the device. Correct. Intune relies on the detection rule (file, registry, or MSI product code) to confirm a successful install. If the rule references the wrong path, key, or version, Intune reports Failed even when the installer succeeded. A. A user-initiated uninstall would normally be reflected after the next device check-in as a removal, not as an installation failure on a successful install. C. An offline device would report a Pending status, not Failed.

Slide 19 · Question 2

You want to deploy a kiosk application only to corporate-owned Windows 11 devices, regardless of who uses them, and you don't want to maintain a separate Microsoft Entra group for each model and OS combination. Which targeting approach is most efficient?

  1. Create a static security group, manually add every kiosk device, and assign the app to that group.
  2. Target all Windows devices with a Required assignment and accept that some non-kiosk devices receive the app.
  3. Assign the app to a broad device group and use an assignment filter for device ownership = Corporate and OS version = Windows 11.
Answer and source explanation

Q5 - Correct: C. Assign the app to a broad device group and use an assignment filter for device ownership = Corporate and OS version = Windows 11. Correct. Assignment filters let you refine targeting based on device properties without creating and maintaining many separate Microsoft Entra groups. A. Static groups require ongoing manual maintenance as devices are added or replaced and don't scale well. B. Over-targeting installs the app on devices that shouldn't have it, which violates the requirement to target only kiosks.

Learning Path 03Module 02

Applicatiebescherming en toegangsbeveiliging

Je beschermt zakelijke gegevens in apps met app protection policies en toepasselijk toegangsbeleid.

← Vorige moduleVolgende module →↑ Terug naar boven

Waarom is deze module belangrijk?

Een apparaat kan persoonlijk of onbeheerd zijn terwijl de organisatie toch grip nodig heeft op zakelijke gegevensstromen.

Leerdoelen

  • Uitleggen: mAM op beheerde en onbeheerde apparaten.
  • Uitleggen: datalekken beperken.
  • Uitleggen: toegang en conditional launch.
  • Uitleggen: toewijzing en probleemonderzoek.

01. MAM op beheerde en onbeheerde apparaten

App protection policies beschermen de zakelijke context in ondersteunde apps. Daarvoor zijn appondersteuning, identiteit en licenties nodig. Volledige MDM-inschrijving is niet in elk scenario vereist. Op een beheerd apparaat kan MAM extra bescherming toevoegen naast apparaatconfiguratie.

02. Datalekken beperken

Stuur kopiëren, plakken, opslaan en het openen van documenten naar toegestane bestemmingen. Vereis waar passend versleuteling en een app-PIN. Denk aan het verschil tussen zakelijke en persoonlijke accounts in dezelfde app. Test echte gebruikershandelingen, zoals een bijlage openen vanuit Outlook in een andere app.

03. Toegang en conditional launch

Access requirements regelen voorwaarden zoals een PIN. Conditional launch reageert op bijvoorbeeld verouderde apps, OS-versies of dreigingssignalen. De actie kan waarschuwen, toegang blokkeren of zakelijke data wissen. Conditional Access stuurt toegang tot resources op basis van ondersteunde signalen en moet zorgvuldig op platform en client worden afgestemd.

04. Toewijzing en probleemonderzoek

Wijs app protection aan de juiste gebruikers toe en controleer de managementstatusfiltering. Onderzoek appversie, ingelogd account, ondersteunde broker en licenties. Lees app-protectionrapportage en Entra-aanmeldlogs. Gebruik selectieve zakelijke wipe bij passend uitdiensttreden of verlies en controleer de terugmelding.

Wat moet je als beheerder onthouden?

  • MAM vraagt ondersteunde apps en de juiste gebruikerscontext.
  • Appbeveiliging en resource-toegang zijn afzonderlijke lagen.
  • Selectieve wipe richt zich op zakelijke appgegevens.

Samenvatting

MAM vraagt ondersteunde apps en de juiste gebruikerscontext. Appbeveiliging en resource-toegang zijn afzonderlijke lagen. Selectieve wipe richt zich op zakelijke appgegevens. Gebruik de hoofdstukken om keuzes te onderbouwen en controleer altijd de effectieve toestand naast de portalstatus.

Cursusverdieping per onderwerp (Engels) · 14 bronslides

Original course content. Text order follows the source presentation; visual tables and diagrams are represented as text.

Slide 22

Understand Mobile Application Management (MAM) and App Protection Policies

  • Two zones on one device : controlled by app protection policies
  • Corporate · managed apps
  • Outlook · Teams · Word · Edge : inside an encrypted MAM container
  • IT applies data-protection, access, and launch policy to these apps only
  • Personal · unmanaged
  • WhatsApp · Safari · Photos · Spotify : IT can't touch them
  • Personal data stays private; the MAM boundary separates the two zones
  • 01 · Data relocation
  • Stop leaks : block copy → personal apps
  • Force links into managed Edge
  • Restrict Save As to corporate cloud
  • 02 · Access requirements
  • Gate the app itself with PIN or biometric
  • Independent of the device lock screen
  • 03 · Conditional launch
  • Refuse to open on a compromised device
  • Jailbroken / rooted, outdated OS, or offline too long
Slide 23

App protection policies in action

  • Copy & paste blocked
  • Word → WhatsApp
  • Pasting corporate text into an unmanaged app is rejected
  • Word → Teams still works : both are managed
  • Links forced to Edge
  • Outlook → Safari
  • A link inside a corporate email opens in managed Edge : not the personal browser
  • Selective wipe
  • Leaver scenario
  • Removes only the corporate data inside managed apps
  • Personal photos and chats stay intact
Slide 24

Plan application protection strategies for BYOD and corporate devices

  • Capability
  • BYOD strategy
  • Corporate strategy
  • Send org data to other apps
  • Policy-managed apps only
  • Policy-managed apps only
  • Save copies of org data
  • Blocked
  • OneDrive / SharePoint only
  • Restrict cut, copy, paste
  • Managed apps with paste in
  • Policy-managed apps
  • Require app PIN to open
  • Required
  • Optional : device PIN suffices
  • Block jailbroken / rooted
  • Block access
  • Block : plus MDM compliance
  • BYOD · MAM-WE
  • User-owned phone : no device enrollment
  • Policy targets the apps, not the device
  • Selective wipe removes only corporate data
  • Corporate · defense-in-depth
  • Company-owned, MDM-enrolled
  • Device hardened by MDM; APP layers on top
  • Enforce both MDM compliance and APP posture
  • Enforce with Conditional Access : Grant control Require app protection policy: Microsoft 365 mobile apps refuse to load corporate data on devices outside the policy.
Slide 25

Configure app protection policies for unenrolled (BYOD) devices

  • 1 · Target unmanaged
  • Apps · App protection policies
  • Target to apps on all device types = No
  • Device types = Unmanaged
  • Platform : iOS/iPadOS and Android
  • 2 · Data protection
  • Send org data to other apps : Policy managed apps
  • Save copies of org data : Block
  • Restrict cut, copy, paste : Managed apps with paste in
  • 3 · Access requirements
  • PIN to access : Require
  • Biometrics instead of PIN : Yes
  • Work account credentials : Not required
  • 4 · Conditional launch
  • Jailbroken / rooted : Block access
  • Offline grace period : block after 12 hours
  • Offline interval before wipe : 90 days
Slide 26

Configure BYOD app protection : enforcement and exit

  • Enforce with Conditional Access
  • Require app protection policy as the grant control
  • Target resources : Office 365
  • Client apps : Mobile apps and desktop clients
  • Microsoft 365 mobile apps refuse to load corporate data on devices outside the policy
  • Exit strategy · selective wipe
  • Apps > App selective wipe > Create wipe request
  • Removes only the corporate data inside managed apps
  • Personal photos, messages, and apps stay intact on the user's phone
Slide 27

Configure app protection policies for enrolled (corporate) devices

  • 1 · Target managed
  • Apps · App protection policies
  • Target to apps on all device types = No
  • Device types = Managed (MDM enrolled)
  • Platform : iOS/iPadOS and Android
  • 2 · Data protection
  • Send org data to other apps : Policy managed apps
  • Save copies of org data : Block
  • Allow save to : OneDrive, SharePoint
  • Cut, copy, paste : Managed apps with paste in
  • 3 · Access requirements
  • PIN for access : Not required
  • Work account credentials : Not required
  • Rationale : MDM already enforces device PIN
  • 4 · Conditional launch
  • Max PIN attempts : 5 (reset via Entra ID)
  • Offline grace : block after 720 min (12 h)
  • Offline grace : wipe after 90 days
  • Jailbroken / rooted : Block
Slide 28

App protection on a managed device : why, and how assignment works

  • Why APP on a managed device
  • MDM enforces device encryption and a strong passcode
  • APP draws the boundary between work apps and personal apps
  • Corporate data can't be copied out of Outlook into WhatsApp or saved to Dropbox
  • How assignment works
  • Assigned to users : evaluated per device
  • Target an Entra ID user group
  • On sign-in, Intune checks device state and applies the Managed policy here, or the Unmanaged (BYOD) policy on a personal phone : seamlessly, in the background
Slide 29

Define data protection, encryption, and app restriction settings

  • Pillar 1 · Data protection
  • Sandbox the data : control how it moves between apps and the clipboard
  • Send org data to other apps : Policy managed apps
  • Save copies of org data : Block (allow OneDrive, SharePoint)
  • Cut, copy, paste : Managed apps with paste in
  • Sync to native contacts app : Block
  • Pillar 2 · Encryption
  • Unreadable on a lost device : at the device and inside the app
  • Windows : BitLocker; keys escrowed to Entra ID
  • macOS : FileVault; recovery key to Intune
  • iOS / Android : passcode triggers hardware encryption
  • App level (MAM) : Encrypt org data: Require
  • Pillar 3 · App restrictions
  • Tighten the boundary with the OS : block easy exfiltration paths
  • Web content transfer : Microsoft Edge
  • Third-party keyboards : Block (prevents keyloggers)
  • Screen capture (Android) : Block
  • Minimum OS / app version : Block if behind baseline
Slide 30

Define access requirements and conditional launch behaviors

  • Front door · access requirements
  • Who is opening the app : and what must they prove?
  • PIN for access : require on BYOD; optional on managed
  • Biometrics (Touch ID / Face ID) : allow; falls back to PIN
  • Work or school credentials : not required for daily use
  • Ongoing health check · conditional launch
  • Is the device still healthy : and does the policy still apply?
  • Jailbroken / rooted : block access or wipe data
  • Min OS / app version : warn, then block access
  • Max PIN attempts : 5 (reset PIN via Entra ID)
  • Offline grace : block after 12 h, wipe after 90 d
  • Disabled Entra ID account : wipe data
Slide 31

Conditional launch : three actions the user actually sees

  • Warn
  • Heads-up only
  • A pop-up appears (“Your iOS version is out of date”)
  • User can dismiss and keep working
  • Block access
  • Door closed, data intact
  • The app opens but content is shielded
  • User must remediate : connect to Wi-Fi, update the OS. Nothing is deleted
  • Wipe data
  • Selective wipe
  • The corporate container's encryption keys are destroyed
  • Work emails, chats, files vanish : personal data is untouched

  • Setting
  • BYOD
  • Corporate
  • Users
  • Workforce group · exclude break-glass
  • Workforce group · exclude break-glass
  • Target resources
  • Office 365
  • All cloud apps · or high-risk subset
  • Device platforms
  • iOS · Android
  • Windows · macOS
  • Client apps
  • Mobile apps and desktop clients
  • Any client
  • Device filter
  • :
  • deviceOwnership -eq "Company"
  • Grant control
  • Require app protection policy
  • Require device marked compliant
  • BYOD scenario · MAM
  • Require app protection policy
  • Block native Mail and Gmail apps
  • Corporate data only flows through managed apps like Outlook
  • Corporate scenario · MDM
  • Require compliant device
  • Link Intune compliance to Entra ID
  • Only encrypted, up-to-date, healthy devices reach corporate data
Slide 33

Assign and monitor app protection policies

  • 1 · Assign
  • Target users : not devices; APP rides with the identity
  • The same person gets the right policy on every phone they sign into
  • Start small : pilot to IT champions before “All Users”
  • Exclusions : break-glass and service accounts always
  • Conflicts : most restrictive setting wins
  • 2 · Monitor
  • Apps > Monitor > App protection status : one source of truth
  • Assigned users : who checked in and received the policy
  • Flagged users : locked out, jailbreak, max PIN exceeded
  • User status : per-user deep dive: apps, devices, sync time
  • 3 · Troubleshoot
  • When policy doesn't apply, walk the checklist first
  • Corporate sign-in : fires only on Entra ID credentials
  • App targeted : is Teams in the public apps list?
  • Device state filter : Managed vs Unmanaged both need a policy
  • Force a sync : profile · settings · Intune diagnostics
Slide 34

Troubleshoot application protection policy issues

  • APP protects a user identity inside an app container : not the OS. Work the playbook top to bottom, not device syncs and hardware logs.
  • Step 0 · Verify the basics
  • Miss any and the policy silently fails to apply
  • Licensed : Intune via EMS or M365 E3/E5
  • Targeted : user group, never device group
  • Supported app : Outlook, Edge, Teams only
  • Corporate sign-in : Entra ID credentials, not personal
  • Step 1 · Infrastructure
  • Intune admin center : Troubleshooting + support
  • Pick the user → App protection status
  • Is the app listed? : if Outlook isn't shown, it never checked in with MAM
  • Confirm the right policy name is targeting them
  • Device type : Managed vs Unmanaged matches scope?
  • Step 2 · Client-side
  • Pull diagnostics from the app : local cache may be stale
  • Edge : URL bar about:intunehelp · View Intune App Status
  • Outlook : Settings · Help & Feedback · Collect Diagnostics
  • Incident ID : bundles logs for a Microsoft support ticket
Slide 35

Troubleshoot app protection : common admin gotchas

  • Most-restrictive merge
  • Two policies on one user
  • The strictest setting wins each field : check for a second policy before re-authoring
  • Missing Conditional Access
  • Users bypass Outlook for native Mail
  • Close the backdoor with a CA policy that requires app protection
  • App not in scope
  • Strict policy on Word but Excel forgotten from public apps
  • Add every app users touch to the policy's managed-apps list

1. Welke instelling beperkt zakelijke gegevens naar persoonlijke apps?

2. Kan MAM aanvullende waarde hebben op een al ingeschreven toestel?

Oorspronkelijke kennisvragen uit de cursus (Engels)

Unchanged question wording from the source decks, apart from typography. Answers and explanations, where present, come from the associated speaker notes. Treat them as course-source answers; validate current product behavior in Microsoft documentation.

Slide 36 · Question 1

Contoso lets employees access corporate email from their personal iPhones but doesn't want IT to manage the entire device. Which Microsoft Intune approach best meets this requirement?

  1. Enroll each personal device in Mobile Device Management (MDM) and apply a device compliance policy.
  2. Deploy Mobile Application Management (MAM) without enrollment by assigning an App Protection Policy to the user's corporate identity.
  3. Block all access to corporate email from personal devices using a Conditional Access policy.
Answer and source explanation

Q1 - Correct: B. Deploy Mobile Application Management (MAM) without enrollment by assigning an App Protection Policy to the user's corporate identity. MAM Without Enrollment (MAM-WE) applies App Protection Policies to the corporate identity inside supported apps without enrolling or managing the personal device. A. Full MDM enrollment gives IT control over the entire personal device, which is exactly what Contoso wants to avoid in a BYOD scenario. C. Blocking access prevents employees from working from their personal devices and doesn't meet the requirement to allow access in a controlled way.

Slide 36 · Question 2

An administrator wants to allow users to copy text from their personal browser into a managed work email but block copying confidential data out of the work email into personal apps. Which App Protection Policy data relocation setting achieves this?

  1. Set Restrict cut, copy, and paste between other apps to Any app.
  2. Set Restrict cut, copy, and paste between other apps to Blocked.
  3. Set Restrict cut, copy, and paste between other apps to Policy managed apps with paste in.
Answer and source explanation

Q2 - Correct: C. Set **Restrict cut, copy, and paste between other apps** to **Policy managed apps with paste in**. This setting allows data to be pasted *into* managed apps from any source while preventing corporate data from being copied *out* to unmanaged apps. A. Allowing any app removes the protection boundary and lets users paste corporate data into personal apps. B. Blocking all cut, copy, and paste hurts the user experience and prevents the desired inbound paste from personal apps.

Slide 36 · Question 3

Why should App Protection Policies be assigned to user groups rather than device groups in Microsoft Intune?

  1. Because App Protection Policies target the corporate identity inside supported apps, not the physical device.
  2. Because device groups can't be created in Microsoft Intune for mobile platforms.
  3. Because user groups apply policies faster than device groups.
Answer and source explanation

Q3 - Correct: A. Because App Protection Policies target the corporate identity inside supported apps, not the physical device. APP travels with the user's Microsoft Entra ID account and is evaluated when the user signs into a supported app, so user group assignment is required. B. Device groups exist in Intune and are commonly used for other policy types, such as device configuration profiles. C. Speed isn't the reason. The assignment requirement is driven by how App Protection Policies evaluate user identity at app sign-in.

Slide 37 · Question 1

Users on BYOD iPhones are still able to read corporate mail in the native iOS Mail app, bypassing the Outlook App Protection Policy. Which control closes this gap?

  1. Add a stricter App PIN requirement to the existing App Protection Policy.
  2. Create a Microsoft Entra Conditional Access policy that requires an app protection policy for Exchange Online.
  3. Increase the conditional launch offline grace period in the App Protection Policy.
Answer and source explanation

Q4 - Correct: B. Create a Microsoft Entra Conditional Access policy that requires an app protection policy for Exchange Online. Conditional Access enforces APP at sign-in by blocking native, unmanaged apps and requiring a supported, policy-managed client like Outlook. A. App PIN settings only affect managed apps. They have no effect on the native iOS Mail client. C. Offline grace periods only affect managed apps after they go offline; they don't prevent users from using native unmanaged apps.

Slide 37 · Question 2

A licensed user reports that the App Protection Policy isn't applying when they sign into Microsoft Outlook on their phone. What should you verify first?

  1. That the user is signing in with their corporate Microsoft Entra ID account, not a personal account.
  2. That the device is enrolled in Microsoft Intune MDM.
  3. That the App Protection Policy is assigned to a device group that contains the phone.
Answer and source explanation

Q5 - Correct: A. That the user is signing in with their corporate Microsoft Entra ID account, not a personal account. App Protection Policies only activate when the user signs in with the corporate identity targeted by the policy; personal accounts in the same app are ignored. B. MDM enrollment isn't required for App Protection Policies; MAM Without Enrollment is supported for BYOD scenarios. C. App Protection Policies must be assigned to user groups, not device groups, so a device group assignment wouldn't apply the policy.

Learning Path 03Module 03

Applicatielevenscyclus en gebruikerservaring

Je beheert software van aanvraag en eerste uitrol tot updates, vervanging en verwijdering.

← Vorige moduleVolgende module →↑ Terug naar boven

Waarom is deze module belangrijk?

Zonder lifecyclebeheer blijven verouderde apps of tegenstrijdige toewijzingen achter en verliezen gebruikers vertrouwen in de softwarecatalogus.

Leerdoelen

  • Uitleggen: eigenaarschap en fasering.
  • Uitleggen: updates en supersedence.
  • Uitleggen: targeting en standaardinstellingen.
  • Uitleggen: gebruik, toegang en uitdienstname.

01. Eigenaarschap en fasering

Leg eigenaar, licentie, supportstatus, doelgroep en versie vast. Begin met een pilot en schaal uit na acceptatie. Informeer gebruikers over zichtbare wijzigingen en herstarts. Zorg voor een onderhouds- en verwijderplan voordat een app breed beschikbaar wordt gesteld.

02. Updates en supersedence

Een opvolgende Win32-app kan een eerdere versie vervangen via supersedence. Bepaal of de vorige versie eerst moet verdwijnen en controleer afhankelijkheden. Niet elke update volgt automatisch dezelfde route; stem het beleid af op de appsoort. Test behoud van gebruikersinstellingen en herstelmogelijkheden.

03. Targeting en standaardinstellingen

Gebruik groepen en ondersteunde filters om software gericht aan te bieden. Houd gebruikers- en apparaattoewijzingen overzichtelijk en documenteer uitzonderingen. Appconfiguratie kan ondersteunde apps vooraf instellen. Dat is een ander doel dan app protection: configuratie bereidt gebruik voor, bescherming begrenst de omgang met data.

04. Gebruik, toegang en uitdienstname

Vergelijk installatiestatus met daadwerkelijk gebruik waar zulke gegevens beschikbaar zijn. Toegang tot bedrijfsresources kan compliance en andere Conditional Access-signalen vereisen. Verwijder ongewenste software gecontroleerd met de juiste intentie. Controleer daarna resterende data, licentievrijgave en het resultaat op de client.

Wat moet je als beheerder onthouden?

  • Iedere app heeft een eigenaar en onderhoudsplan nodig.
  • Supersedence vraagt expliciete vervangingskeuzes.
  • Appconfiguratie en appbescherming dienen verschillende doelen.

Samenvatting

Iedere app heeft een eigenaar en onderhoudsplan nodig. Supersedence vraagt expliciete vervangingskeuzes. Appconfiguratie en appbescherming dienen verschillende doelen. Gebruik de hoofdstukken om keuzes te onderbouwen en controleer altijd de effectieve toestand naast de portalstatus.

Cursusverdieping per onderwerp (Engels) · 9 bronslides

Original course content. Text order follows the source presentation; visual tables and diagrams are represented as text.

Slide 40

Understand application lifecycle management in Microsoft Intune

  • Every app moves through the same managed lifecycle in Intune : from creation to retirement.
  • 1 Add
  • Create the app as a managed object in Intune
  • 2 Deploy
  • Assign to users or devices based on business need
  • 3 Configure
  • Predefine settings for a consistent experience
  • 4 Protect
  • Secure org data with access and app protection
  • 5 Retire
  • Remove or replace apps no longer needed
Slide 41

Update and retire applications in Microsoft Intune

  • The update and retirement path depends on app type, platform, assignment intent, and whether you remove the app or only corporate data.
  • Microsoft Store apps
  • Kept current through Microsoft Store or Intune, depending on app type and assignment
  • Win32 apps
  • Use supersedence to update or replace an older Win32 app with a newer version or successor
  • LOB & mobile apps
  • Upload newer packages for LOB; mobile store apps update through Apple or Managed Google Play
  • Enterprise App Catalog
  • Provides prepared Win32 apps and update candidates : review, create the newer app, use supersedence
  • Retirement & removal
  • Uninstall assignment removes apps that Intune installed · selective wipe removes corporate app data in BYOD/MAM · device Retire removes company data, profiles, settings, and many managed apps. Retirement keeps the estate clean and reduces support, security, and governance risk.
Slide 42

Assign applications using groups, filters, and targeting

  • After an app is added, assignments determine who receives it, how it's delivered, and whether targeting is refined by filters.
  • Groups
  • Start with broad targets : users, devices, All users, All devices
  • Include and exclude groups to shape the deployment population
  • Assignment intent
  • Required installs automatically
  • Available lets users install from Company Portal
  • Uninstall removes the app when Intune installed it
  • Filters
  • Refine the group by managed device or managed app properties
  • OS version, device category, ownership, enrollment profile, application version
Slide 43

Assign applications : workflow and targeting guidance

  • Assignment workflow
  • 1 Open
  • Apps > All apps and select the app
  • 2 Edit
  • Properties > Assignments
  • 3 Choose intent
  • Required, Available (with/without enrollment), or Uninstall
  • 4 Add groups
  • Include or Exclude, apply filters when needed, and save
  • Targeting guidance
  • User assignment follows the person across devices
  • Device assignment targets managed devices only
  • Use filters when a group is too broad
  • Pilot assignments before broad deployment
Slide 44

Manage user and device groups for application delivery

  • Groups organize the delivery model : target roles, departments, device types, and shared-device scenarios without assigning apps one by one.
  • User groups
  • Choose when app access should follow people across their enrolled devices
  • A strong fit for job roles, departments, and productivity apps
  • Example : Clinical Staff receives a secure messaging app as Required
  • Device groups
  • Choose when the app must be present on specific hardware
  • Kiosks, shared tablets, labs, or exam-room devices
  • Example : Exam Room Tablets get a check-in app as Required, independent of who signs in
  • Assignment intents
  • Available : user installs from Company Portal
  • Required : Intune installs automatically
  • Uninstall : Intune removes the app from targets
  • Best practice
  • Review memberships regularly, test with pilot groups, and use dynamic Microsoft Entra groups where membership should follow attributes automatically.
Slide 45

Configure default application settings and user experience

  • App configuration policies apply predefined settings before users run an app : reducing manual setup, support issues, and inconsistent behavior.
  • Managed devices
  • Uses the MDM operating-system channel for enrolled devices
  • Common scenarios : account setup, work/school account restrictions, S/MIME
  • App-specific settings for iOS/iPadOS and Android
  • Managed apps
  • Uses the MAM channel : does not require enrollment
  • Works with apps that use the Intune App SDK or wrapping tool
  • Supports iOS/iPadOS, Android, and Microsoft Edge for Windows
  • Settings commonly configured
  • Security, authentication, and encryption requirements
  • Account, connectivity, VPN, proxy, or port settings
  • Branding, language, regional, and feature defaults
  • Operational best practices
  • Start with vendor documentation for supported keys
  • Pilot changes and monitor configuration status
  • Document settings and avoid duplicate configuration paths
Slide 46

Enforce compliance requirements for applications

  • Application compliance combines app protection policies, Conditional Access, and device compliance to control access to work data inside supported apps.
  • App protection policy
  • Protects org data at the app layer
  • Applies on enrolled and unenrolled devices when the app supports the Intune SDK or wrapping tool
  • Conditional Access
  • Enforces the requirement at sign-in
  • Access can be blocked when the app is not protected or conditions aren't met
  • Device compliance
  • Adds device-health conditions when needed
  • Platform, encryption, jailbreak/root detection, or minimum OS requirements
Slide 47

Enforce application compliance : managed-app controls and enforcement flow

  • Managed-app controls
  • Access requirements : PIN, biometrics, or corporate credentials
  • Conditional launch : warn, block, or wipe corporate data
  • Data protection : restrict copy/paste, save-as, backups, and unmanaged locations
  • Common enforcement flow
  • 1 Open
  • User opens a protected app
  • 2 Evaluate
  • Conditional Access evaluates access requirements
  • 3 Check policy
  • The app checks for an applicable policy
  • 4 Decide
  • Access granted, blocked, or corporate data wiped
Slide 48

Monitor app lifecycle and usage analytics

  • Core Intune reports
  • App overview : device and user installation status
  • Discovered apps : software inventory by app, platform, version, device count, publisher
  • App protection status : policy state for users and app instances
  • Endpoint analytics
  • Application reliability : desktop app usage and failure info over the last 14 days
  • Aggregate usage duration, crash counts, and mean time to failure
  • Microsoft Intune admin center paths
  • Report
  • Admin center path
  • Application reliability
  • Reports > Endpoint analytics > Application reliability
  • Discovered apps
  • Apps > Monitor > Discovered apps
  • App protection status
  • Apps > Monitor > App protection status

1. Waarvoor dient supersedence bij Win32-apps?

2. Wat hoort bij uitfaseren van software?

Oorspronkelijke kennisvragen uit de cursus (Engels)

Unchanged question wording from the source decks, apart from typography. Answers and explanations, where present, come from the associated speaker notes. Treat them as course-source answers; validate current product behavior in Microsoft documentation.

Slide 49 · Question 1

A Windows administrator uploads a new package for a line-of-business app to Intune, but Intune rejects the update. What is the most likely cause?

  1. The new package has the same version number as the package already stored in Intune.
  2. Line-of-business apps can only be updated through the Enterprise App Catalog.
  3. Required line-of-business apps stop receiving updates after the first install.
Answer and source explanation

Q1 - Correct: A. The new package has the same version number as the package already stored in Intune. For line-of-business apps, Intune extracts the version from each package and rejects updates when the version matches the version already stored, so the package must be genuinely newer. B. Line-of-business apps are updated by uploading a newer package, not through the Enterprise App Catalog. C. Required line-of-business apps continue to receive updates when a genuinely newer package is uploaded; the rejection is caused by a duplicate version.

Slide 49 · Question 2

An organization wants to enforce that users on personal, unenrolled iPhones cannot copy data from Outlook into personal apps. Which Intune capability is the best fit?

  1. An app protection policy that includes data protection settings.
  2. A device compliance policy targeting personal iPhones.
  3. An app configuration policy for managed devices.
Answer and source explanation

Q2 - Correct: A. An app protection policy that includes data protection settings. App protection policies apply at the app layer, work on unenrolled devices, and include data protection settings that restrict actions such as copy and paste between managed and personal apps. B. Device compliance policies require device enrollment and don't control in-app data movement. C. Managed devices app configuration policies require enrollment and configure app settings, not data protection actions like copy and paste.

Slide 49 · Question 3

You want to deploy a Win32 app to only the subset of devices in a broad group that are running a specific Windows version. What's the most efficient way to refine the targeting?

  1. Apply an assignment filter for managed devices when assigning the app to the group.
  2. Create a new Microsoft Entra ID group for every Windows version in the environment.
  3. Assign the app as Available so users self-select the correct version.
Answer and source explanation

Q3 - Correct: A. Apply an assignment filter for managed devices when assigning the app to the group. Assignment filters refine a group's target by device properties such as OS version, manufacturer, or enrollment profile without creating new groups. B. Creating a group per OS version is hard to maintain; filters are designed exactly for this scenario. C. Changing the intent to Available doesn't restrict the assignment to a specific OS version and shifts the decision to users.

Slide 50 · Question 1

An administrator confirms an app is installed on a user's iPhone but the user still can't open corporate email in the app. App deployment shows Installed. What should the administrator check next?

  1. App protection status under Apps > Monitor to see whether the required app protection policy is applied.
  2. The Discovered apps report to confirm the app exists on the device.
  3. The Endpoint analytics Application reliability report.
Answer and source explanation

Q4 - Correct: A. App protection status under Apps > Monitor to see whether the required app protection policy is applied. App deployment and app protection are separate; an app can be Installed but still blocked from corporate data when the required app protection policy isn't applied. B. Discovered apps is an inventory report; it doesn't explain protection-related blocks on corporate data access. C. Application reliability tracks crashes and performance for desktop apps, not mobile app protection enforcement.

Slide 50 · Question 2

An organization is replacing an older Win32 line-of-business app with a newer version and wants devices to move to the new app cleanly. Which Intune feature is designed for this?

  1. Supersedence between the older and newer Win32 apps.
  2. Assigning the new app with the Available intent only.
  3. Adding both apps to a single Microsoft Entra ID device group.
Answer and source explanation

Q5 - Correct: A. Supersedence between the older and newer Win32 apps. Win32 supersedence lets a newer Win32 app update or replace an older Win32 app, which is the supported path for this exact scenario. B. An Available assignment doesn't replace the older app or move installed devices to the new version. C. Group assignment alone doesn't establish the relationship needed to update or replace the older app.

Learning Path 03Module 04

Applicatieprestaties monitoren en verbeteren

Je onderscheidt installatiesucces van runtimebetrouwbaarheid en maakt rapporten bruikbaar voor verbetering.

← Vorige moduleVolgende module →↑ Terug naar boven

Waarom is deze module belangrijk?

Een app die overal geïnstalleerd is kan nog steeds crashen en veel productiviteit kosten.

Leerdoelen

  • Uitleggen: installatie en gebruik apart meten.
  • Uitleggen: rapporten voor de juiste doelgroep.
  • Uitleggen: endpoint analytics inzetten.
  • Uitleggen: verbeteren en terugmeten.

01. Installatie en gebruik apart meten

App deployment status laat zien of distributie gelukt is. Application reliability richt zich op gedrag tijdens gebruik. Combineer aantallen, percentages en de meetperiode. Een hoog foutenpercentage bij een kleine groep heeft een andere impact dan een beperkt percentage bij duizenden gebruikers.

02. Rapporten voor de juiste doelgroep

Operationele rapporten ondersteunen directe opvolging van fouten. Organisatorische rapporten tonen trends en totalen voor planning en management. Meer specialistische analyse gebruikt exports of aanvullende gegevens. Kies alleen gegevens die de vraag beantwoorden en vermijd onnodige verspreiding van persoons- of apparaatinformatie.

03. Endpoint analytics inzetten

Gebruik appbetrouwbaarheid om veelvoorkomende crashes te herkennen en segmenteer op appversie, OS of hardware. Onderzoek correlaties met recente changes. Telemetrie geeft een richting, geen automatisch bewijs van oorzaak. Reproduceer het probleem of vergelijk met een passende controlegroep voordat je terugrolt.

04. Verbeteren en terugmeten

Prioriteer op getroffen gebruikers en bedrijfsimpact. Test een nieuw pakket, aangepaste versie of gerichte instelling bij een kleine groep. Meet daarna dezelfde indicatoren over een vergelijkbare periode. Documenteer het effect en maak van succesvolle correcties een herhaalbaar beheerproces.

Wat moet je als beheerder onthouden?

  • Geïnstalleerd betekent niet stabiel.
  • Vergelijk gelijke meetperioden en doelgroepen.
  • Koppel iedere analyse aan een concrete actie en effectmeting.

Samenvatting

Geïnstalleerd betekent niet stabiel. Vergelijk gelijke meetperioden en doelgroepen. Koppel iedere analyse aan een concrete actie en effectmeting. Gebruik de hoofdstukken om keuzes te onderbouwen en controleer altijd de effectieve toestand naast de portalstatus.

Cursusverdieping per onderwerp (Engels) · 8 bronslides

Original course content. Text order follows the source presentation; visual tables and diagrams are represented as text.

Slide 53

Understand App Health and Performance

  • Why baselines matter
  • Compare metrics against organizational averages, not in isolation
  • A sudden spike (1 crash/month → 10 crashes/week) = an actionable problem
  • Prevents alert fatigue from false positives
  • Key app health metrics
  • Metric
  • What it reveals
  • Installation success rate
  • Whether apps deploy correctly to targeted devices
  • Crash frequency
  • Stability issues : mean time between failures
  • Startup impact score
  • Seconds added to device boot time per app
  • Resource consumption
  • CPU/memory usage affecting other apps
Slide 54

App health : the hidden cost, and modern monitoring

  • Hidden cost of ignoring performance
  • Undetected installation failures → unprotected or unlicensed devices
  • A 5-second startup delay × 1,000 daily reboots = hours of lost productivity/week
  • Users work around broken apps → shadow IT + licensing waste
  • Traditional vs. modern monitoring
  • Traditional
  • Modern (Intune)
  • Test once before deployment
  • Continuous production monitoring
  • Controlled lab environment
  • Real-world diverse conditions
  • Reactive : users report issues
  • Proactive : identify before escalation
  • Manual device checks
  • Automated telemetry collection
Slide 55

Monitor App Deployment Status

  • Device install status
  • Per-device list : device name, user, platform, version, status detail, last check-in
  • User install status
  • Aggregated per-user : shows coverage across a user's multiple devices
  • Installation status : what it means
  • Status
  • Meaning
  • Installed
  • Successfully deployed and functional
  • Not Installed
  • Targeted but not yet present
  • Failed
  • Encountered errors during deployment
  • Install Pending
  • Initiated but awaiting restart or user action
  • Not Applicable
  • Platform mismatch or compatibility issue
Slide 56

Monitor app deployment : status reporting and filters

  • Status reporting by app type
  • App type
  • Status reporting
  • Store apps (Available)
  • Only reported if the user installs
  • Required / Win32 / LOB
  • Always detailed status
  • Managed Google Play
  • Includes version detail
  • Filters and search
  • Filter by platform, status, device name, or username
  • Check the last check-in time : a stale “pending” is different from an active failure
  • Read the timestamp before you troubleshoot a failure
Slide 57

Track Installation Success and Failure Reports

  • Rates beat raw counts
  • 100 failures across 10,000 devices = a 1% rate that might be acceptable
  • 5 failures out of 25 attempts = a 20% rate demanding immediate attention
  • Many failures happen during download, before install even attempts
  • User- vs IT-actionable
  • User-actionable : declined permissions, low storage: educate and self-serve
  • IT-actionable : corrupt packages, incompatible versions, missing dependencies
  • After fixes, monitor reports to confirm remediation succeeded
  • AOSP error codes : who resolves them
  • AOSP error code
  • Description
  • User-actionable
  • 0x87D54FB0
  • User didn't allow install or declined permissions
  • Yes : educate users to accept prompts
  • 0x87D54FB1
  • Android OS rejected the installation
  • No : problem with the APK or device config
  • 0x87D54FB7
  • Device lacks sufficient storage space
  • Yes : user can free storage to resolve
  • 0x87D54FB4
  • APK file is corrupt or invalid
  • No : IT must upload a corrected package
Slide 58

Use Endpoint Analytics to measure app performance

  • Endpoint Analytics continuously monitors device health, startup performance, application reliability, and user-experience metrics across your managed fleet : focusing on user-centric metrics.
  • Application Reliability
  • Shows which applications crash most frequently across your device fleet
  • Reveals how those crashes impact users
  • Startup Performance
  • Device-level boot time measurements
  • Breaks down which applications contribute most to startup delays
  • Baselines
  • Aggregates metrics across all enrolled devices to define what “normal” looks like
  • Success requires acting on the issues identified to improve those baselines
Slide 59

Use Intune reporting tools to identify and resolve performance issues

  • Intune provides a reporting framework that turns raw device and application data into actionable intelligence.
  • Category
  • Purpose
  • Use case
  • Operational
  • Immediate action
  • Active failures right now
  • Organizational
  • Broad summaries for leadership
  • Monthly deployment success rates
  • Historical
  • Trends over time
  • Device age vs. failure rate
  • Specialist
  • Custom raw-data analysis
  • Correlate failures with other variables
  • Reading reports well
  • Every report includes export : download data for analysis outside the admin center
  • Devices check in on schedules : they don't continuously stream telemetry
  • Many reports offer device or user perspectives : the choice affects interpretation
Slide 60

Optimize user experience with actionable insights

  • Monitoring generates data, but data alone doesn't improve user experience : turn insights into concrete actions that resolve problems and prevent issues.
  • Prioritize with limited IT resources
  • Scope : how many users are affected?
  • Severity : how big is the impact?
  • Remediation difficulty : how complex is the fix?
  • Business criticality : how does this affect the business?
  • Communicate the fix
  • Communicating fixes builds trust, encourages future problem reporting, and demonstrates IT responsiveness : but keep communication targeted to the affected audience.

Oorspronkelijke kennisvragen uit de cursus (Engels)

Unchanged question wording from the source decks, apart from typography. Answers and explanations, where present, come from the associated speaker notes. Treat them as course-source answers; validate current product behavior in Microsoft documentation.

Slide 61 · Question 1

You're investigating user complaints that Microsoft Teams crashes frequently on Contoso devices. Which Endpoint Analytics report should you open first to confirm whether Teams is among the most-impacted applications across your fleet?

  1. Startup Performance report
  2. Application Reliability report
  3. App Install Status report
Answer and source explanation

Q1 - Correct: B. Application Reliability report Correct. The Application Reliability report ranks applications by crash count and crash rate across your device fleet, letting you quickly see whether Teams is a top contributor and how many devices it affects. A. Startup Performance measures how long apps add to device boot time, not how often they crash during use. C. App Install Status focuses on whether apps installed successfully, not on runtime stability or crashes.

Slide 61 · Question 2

Your CIO asks for a monthly summary of overall application deployment success across the organization to share with executive leadership. Which Intune report category best fits this request?

  1. Operational
  2. Specialist
  3. Organizational
Answer and source explanation

Q2 - Correct: C. Organizational Correct. Organizational reports provide broader summaries intended for managers and planners, which is the right fit for a monthly executive view of overall deployment success. A. Operational reports surface timely, targeted data for immediate action : like investigating an active failure : not high-level summaries for leadership. B. Specialist reports are custom analyses built on raw data exports, typically used for ad-hoc investigations rather than monthly executive summaries.

Slide 61 · Question 3

You want to enable Endpoint Analytics for the Windows devices in your tenant. Which prerequisite must be in place for devices to contribute telemetry to the Application Reliability and Startup Performance reports?

  1. Devices must have Microsoft Defender for Endpoint Plan 2 licensed.
  2. The Connected User Experiences and Telemetry service must be enabled and running on each device.
  3. Devices must be co-managed with Configuration Manager.
Answer and source explanation

Q3 - Correct: B. The Connected User Experiences and Telemetry service must be enabled and running on each device. Correct. If the Connected User Experiences and Telemetry service is disabled by policy, devices can't upload telemetry and won't appear in Endpoint Analytics reports. A. Endpoint Analytics doesn't depend on Defender for Endpoint Plan 2. The basic Application Reliability and Startup Performance reports are included with standard Microsoft Intune licensing. C. Co-management is supported but not required. Intune-enrolled Windows devices on supported editions can contribute telemetry on their own.

Slide 62 · Question 1

You want to correlate Intune app failure data with sign-in logs and network telemetry to look for patterns the built-in reports don't expose. What's the most appropriate path?

  1. Configure diagnostic settings to send Intune data to an Azure Monitor Log Analytics workspace and query with KQL.
  2. Export each Intune report to CSV every day and manually join them in Excel.
  3. Enable Endpoint Analytics anomaly detection and rely on its built-in alerts.
Answer and source explanation

Q4 - Correct: A. Configure diagnostic settings to send Intune data to an Azure Monitor Log Analytics workspace and query with KQL. Correct. Sending Intune diagnostic data to a Log Analytics workspace lets you write Kusto queries that join app failure data with sign-in logs and other Azure sources to surface correlations the built-in reports can't show. B. Manual CSV joins don't scale, aren't repeatable, and can't easily incorporate live sign-in or network telemetry. Azure Monitor is the supported integration point. C. Endpoint Analytics anomaly detection (Intune Suite / Plan 2) surfaces device-experience anomalies but doesn't join Intune data with arbitrary Azure log sources.

Slide 62 · Question 2

An application shows a high startup impact score in Endpoint Analytics, adding roughly 20 seconds to boot time on hundreds of devices. The vendor confirms the app doesn't need to load at startup for users to be productive. What's the most effective first action?

  1. Replace the affected devices with newer hardware to compensate for the startup delay.
  2. Reconfigure the application so it doesn't auto-launch at startup, and validate the boot-time improvement in Endpoint Analytics afterward.
  3. Disable the Connected User Experiences and Telemetry service to stop reporting the metric.
Answer and source explanation

Q5 - Correct: B. Reconfigure the application so it doesn't auto-launch at startup, and validate the boot-time improvement in Endpoint Analytics afterward. Correct. Removing unnecessary startup loading immediately reduces boot time across all affected devices. Re-checking Endpoint Analytics afterward confirms the improvement and proves the value of the change to stakeholders. A. Hardware refresh is expensive and unnecessary when the root cause is an avoidable startup load, not insufficient device capability. C. Disabling telemetry only hides the metric : it doesn't fix the underlying performance impact users experience.

Learning Path 03Module 05

Enterprise App Catalog beheren

Je gebruikt vooraf voorbereide apppakketten uit Enterprise App Management zonder het eigen beheerproces over te slaan.

← Vorige moduleVolgende module →↑ Terug naar boven

Waarom is deze module belangrijk?

Cataloguspakketten besparen verpakkingswerk, maar doelgroep, updatekeuzes en verificatie blijven verantwoordelijkheid van de organisatie.

Leerdoelen

  • Uitleggen: catalogus en rechten.
  • Uitleggen: installatie en detectie.
  • Uitleggen: updates en vervanging.
  • Uitleggen: rapporten en opvolging.

01. Catalogus en rechten

Enterprise App Management biedt een catalogus met voorbereide Win32-apps. Controleer de vereiste aanvullende licenties en of de gewenste leverancier, versie en taal beschikbaar zijn. Een catalogusvermelding vervangt geen softwarelicentie van de leverancier. Beoordeel of het pakket bij de organisatie past.

02. Installatie en detectie

De catalogus levert onder meer installatie- en detectie-instellingen. Beoordeel die vóór toewijzing en controleer requirements, context en eventuele aanpassingen. Een vooraf ingevulde regel is geen bewijs dat jouw applicatiescenario volledig gedekt is. Test installatie én verwijdering op een representatieve pilot.

03. Updates en vervanging

Volg nieuwe catalogusversies en bepaal wanneer ze worden uitgerold. Controleer de aangeboden update- en supersedenceopties in de tenant in plaats van volledige automatische vervanging te veronderstellen. Stem planning af op kwetsbaarheden, gebruik en releasevalidatie. Bewaar een terugvalroute waar technisch mogelijk.

04. Rapporten en opvolging

Gebruik appstatus en Managed Apps-informatie om uitrol en versies te volgen. Controleer zowel het technische resultaat als de gebruikerservaring. Onderzoek fouten met dezelfde Win32-beheerprincipes als bij eigen pakketten. Leg vast welke onderdelen van het pakket je organisatie heeft aangepast.

Wat moet je als beheerder onthouden?

  • Catalogusbeheer vervangt geen leverancierslicentie.
  • Valideer vooraf ingevulde instellingen.
  • Updates blijven een gecontroleerde beheerbeslissing.

Samenvatting

Catalogusbeheer vervangt geen leverancierslicentie. Valideer vooraf ingevulde instellingen. Updates blijven een gecontroleerde beheerbeslissing. Gebruik de hoofdstukken om keuzes te onderbouwen en controleer altijd de effectieve toestand naast de portalstatus.

Cursusverdieping per onderwerp (Engels) · 4 bronslides

Original course content. Text order follows the source presentation; visual tables and diagrams are represented as text.

Slide 65

Discover and deploy apps from the catalog

  • Enterprise App Catalog
  • Prepackaged Win32 apps : commands, detection, and requirements built in
  • Deploy from the catalog
  • Browse the catalog for prepackaged Win32 apps
  • Install/uninstall commands, detection rules, and requirements are already built in
  • Select an app, then assign it to user or device groups
  • Confirm metadata before publishing the app to users
  • Before you publish
  • Review preconfigured requirements and detection rules instead of assuming they match every environment
  • Use a pilot group before broad deployment when you change imported defaults
Slide 66

Configure default install and detection settings

  • Catalog apps arrive with prebuilt defaults, but reliable deployment depends on reviewing install behavior and detection logic before assignment.
  • Review install behavior
  • Confirm command lines, install context, restart behavior, time limit, and return codes
  • Keep overrides minimal unless the package or environment requires a change
  • Validate detection
  • Check that file, registry, or MSI rules match the intended app version
  • Detection controls status reporting, remediation decisions, and update behavior
Slide 67

Monitor updates and supersedence

  • Catalog apps are not updated automatically when a newer version appears : monitor available updates and use supersedence to move devices forward.
  • Self-updating apps
  • Some catalog apps update through the vendor updater
  • Intune validates and reports the detected version instead of delivering the newer package itself
  • Update workflow
  • Apps > Enterprise App Catalog apps with updates
  • Select the app → Update → Supersede app to link it to the previous version
  • Set assignments, review, create, then monitor status
  • How supersedence helps
  • Updates an older version or replaces a different Win32 app
  • Can uninstall the previous version when required
  • Keeps the version transition visible as a lifecycle step
  • Monitor after update
  • Use app status and device-level reports to confirm the newer version is installed and that older versions are no longer present where expected.
Slide 68

Use Managed Apps report for insights

  • Managed Apps report
  • A device-level view of app installation and availability
  • What it shows
  • Key details : app name, version, resolved intent, and installation status
  • Verify whether an Enterprise App Catalog app reached the intended device
  • Troubleshoot when an app is missing, outdated, failed, or only available
  • Validate updates and supersedence during version transitions
  • How to use it
  • Access from Devices > All devices > select device > Managed Apps
  • Identify older apps that may still exist during retirement planning
  • Compare status across pilot devices before broad deployment

1. Wat neemt Enterprise App Catalog vooral uit handen?

2. Wat doe je met een vooraf ingevulde detectieregel?

Oorspronkelijke kennisvragen uit de cursus (Engels)

Unchanged question wording from the source decks, apart from typography. Answers and explanations, where present, come from the associated speaker notes. Treat them as course-source answers; validate current product behavior in Microsoft documentation.

Slide 69 · Question 1

Your IT team has limited time to manually package Win32 apps and wants a faster way to onboard common business applications on managed Windows devices. Which capability of the Microsoft Intune Enterprise App Catalog directly addresses this need?

  1. It provides prepackaged Win32 apps with preconfigured install and uninstall commands, detection rules, and requirements, so administrators don't have to build each package from scratch.
  2. It replaces Microsoft Entra ID group assignments by targeting apps directly to individual user accounts.
  3. It deploys the same Win32 packages to Linux and macOS endpoints in addition to Windows.
Answer and source explanation

Q1 - Correct: A. It provides prepackaged Win32 apps with preconfigured install and uninstall commands, detection rules, and requirements, so administrators don't have to build each package from scratch. Correct. The Enterprise App Catalog ships ready-to-deploy Win32 app packages where Intune prepopulates install/uninstall commands, restart behavior, detection rules, and requirements. That removes most of the manual packaging effort and accelerates app onboarding for common business applications. B. Incorrect. Catalog apps are still assigned through Intune using existing user or device groups (typically Microsoft Entra ID groups). The catalog reduces packaging effort : it doesn't change how assignments are scoped. C. Incorrect. Enterprise App Catalog apps are prepackaged Win32 applications targeted at managed Windows devices. They aren't a cross-platform deployment mechanism for Linux or macOS.

Slide 69 · Question 2

You add a new app from the Enterprise App Catalog and notice that several fields are already filled in before you reach the Assignments page. Which settings does Intune prepopulate from the catalog package?

  1. Install and uninstall commands, restart behavior, detection rules, and requirements such as supported OS version and architecture.
  2. Device compliance policies, BitLocker encryption settings, and Conditional Access rules for the app.
  3. Support contact details, organizational branding, and end-user license terms displayed in the Company Portal.
Answer and source explanation

Q2 - Correct: A. Install and uninstall commands, restart behavior, detection rules, and requirements such as supported OS version and architecture. Correct. The catalog package supplies the deployment metadata Intune needs to install and verify a Win32 app : install/uninstall commands, restart behavior, detection rules, and requirement rules. You can still override these defaults when your environment needs it. B. Incorrect. Compliance policies, disk encryption, and Conditional Access are configured separately in Intune and Microsoft Entra ID. The catalog package only prepopulates the app's deployment settings, not tenant-wide security policies. C. Incorrect. Company Portal branding and support contact information are tenant-level Intune settings, not values supplied by an individual catalog app package. The catalog focuses on the technical deployment configuration.

Slide 69 · Question 3

A newer version of an Enterprise App Catalog app you previously deployed has just appeared in the catalog. What is the expected way to deliver that newer version to devices that still have the older app installed?

  1. Open the app from the Enterprise App Catalog apps with updates view and use the guided update flow, which creates a new app with the latest package and configures a supersedence relationship with the existing app.
  2. Wait for Intune to automatically silently replace the older app on every assigned device with no administrator action required.
  3. Instruct end users to uninstall the old app manually from Settings and then reinstall the new version from the Company Portal.
Answer and source explanation

Q3 - Correct: A. Open the app from the Enterprise App Catalog apps with updates view and use the guided update flow, which creates a new app with the latest package and configures a supersedence relationship with the existing app. Correct. For non-self-updating catalog apps, Intune surfaces available updates in the admin center and provides a guided flow that creates a new superseding app. This lets you track and assign the new version cleanly while replacing the older one through supersedence. B. Incorrect. Enterprise App Catalog apps aren't automatically updated just because a newer version appears in the catalog. An administrator must initiate the update : typically by creating a superseding app : except for self-updating apps where the vendor's own mechanism delivers the new version. C. Incorrect. Relying on end users to uninstall and reinstall apps is error-prone, inconsistent, and bypasses Intune's tracking. Supersedence is the supported way to transition devices to a newer catalog app version.
