Learning Path 04

Apparaten beveiligen

6 modules · Nederlands met Engelstalige cursusverdieping

Learning Path 04Module 01

Microsoft Defender for Endpoint implementeren

Je verbindt apparaatbeheer met dreigingsdetectie, onboarding en incidentrespons.

← Vorige moduleVolgende module →↑ Terug naar boven

Waarom is deze module belangrijk?

Een geconfigureerd apparaat heeft nog steeds detectie en opvolging nodig wanneer aanvallen instellingen omzeilen of misbruiken.

Leerdoelen

  • Uitleggen: beheer en detectie verbinden.
  • Uitleggen: onboarding en EDR.
  • Uitleggen: instellingen en baselines.
  • Uitleggen: incidenten onderzoeken.

01. Beheer en detectie verbinden

Intune configureert endpoints; Defender for Endpoint levert endpointdetectie en respons. De integratie kan dreigingssignalen delen met compliance. Controleer tenantverbinding, platformondersteuning en licenties. Een ingeschreven Intune-apparaat is niet automatisch succesvol onboarded in Defender.

02. Onboarding en EDR

Gebruik passend onboarding- en Endpoint Detection and Response-beleid voor het platform. Rol uit via een pilot en controleer sensorcommunicatie en zichtbaarheid in Defender. Maak onderscheid tussen een toegewezen policy en een actieve, gezonde sensor. Leg ook het offboardingproces vast.

03. Instellingen en baselines

Beheer Defender-instellingen, endpointsecuritybeleid en baselines met duidelijke doelgroepen. Vermijd overlap met bestaande antivirus- of configuratiebronnen. Een baseline is een uitgangspunt dat je op toepasselijkheid test. Documenteer uitzonderingen en voorkom brede uitsluitingen die detectie onnodig verzwakken.

04. Incidenten onderzoeken

Gebruik Microsoft Defender XDR om alerts in samenhang te beoordelen. Controleer entiteiten, tijdlijn en impact voordat je ingrijpt. Responsacties zoals isolatie kunnen bedrijfsprocessen raken en vereisen de juiste bevoegdheid. Verifieer na herstel sensorstatus, beveiligingsniveau en het verdwijnen van de oorzaak.

Wat moet je als beheerder onthouden?

  • Verifieer onboarding in Defender zelf.
  • EDR is meer dan antivirusconfiguratie.
  • Gebruik dreigingssignalen in een gecontroleerde toegangsstrategie.

Samenvatting

Verifieer onboarding in Defender zelf. EDR is meer dan antivirusconfiguratie. Gebruik dreigingssignalen in een gecontroleerde toegangsstrategie. Gebruik de hoofdstukken om keuzes te onderbouwen en controleer altijd de effectieve toestand naast de portalstatus.

Cursusverdieping per onderwerp (Engels) · 6 bronslides

Original course content. Text order follows the source presentation; visual tables and diagrams are represented as text.

Slide 5

Understand Defender for Endpoint and its role in endpoint security

  • Microsoft Defender for Endpoint
  • Cloud platform for prevention, detection, investigation, and response
  • Endpoint sensors
  • Collect process, network, file, registry, and user activity from devices
  • Minimal impact on the user
  • Cloud service
  • Analyzes device signals in the cloud
  • Uses Microsoft threat intelligence, machine learning, and behavioral analytics
  • Integration points
  • Correlates with Microsoft Defender XDR
  • Feeds Intune and Entra Conditional Access
  • Includes threat and vulnerability management
  • Beyond traditional antivirus
  • A cloud-based platform using endpoint telemetry, threat intelligence, behavioral analytics, and cloud protection across Windows, macOS, Linux, Android, and iOS/iPadOS : adding EDR, ASR, advanced hunting, AIR, and vulnerability insights.
Slide 6

Onboard devices to Defender for Endpoint using Intune

  • The onboarding method depends on the platform : all routed through Microsoft Intune.
  • Windows
  • Recommended: an Intune Endpoint detection and response policy
  • Use a preconfigured policy for fast onboarding
  • Or a custom policy for more control
  • macOS
  • Deploy the Microsoft Defender app and onboarding package
  • Add required profiles: system extensions, network filtering, full disk access, notifications, and preferences
  • Android / iOS / iPadOS
  • Deploy the Microsoft Defender app
  • Add app and device configuration policies
  • Lets mobile threat signals flow to Defender
Slide 7

Configure Defender for Endpoint settings and security baselines

  • Create and deploy a security baseline
  • Go to Endpoint security > Security baselines
  • Create a Microsoft Defender for Endpoint Security Baseline profile
  • Review default settings and customize only when required
  • Pilot before broad deployment to catch productivity or app issues
  • Monitor profile status, device status, and conflicts
  • Purpose-built endpoint security policies
  • Antivirus
  • Tamper Protection
  • Attack surface reduction
  • Firewall
  • Endpoint detection and response (EDR)
  • Use the Endpoint security node for focused, purpose-built policies.
Slide 8

Configure Endpoint Detection and Response (EDR) policies

  • Endpoint detection and response policy
  • Onboards devices to your Defender for Endpoint tenant
  • What it does
  • Configures devices to send security telemetry to the Defender for Endpoint tenant
  • Deploys tenant-specific onboarding settings, service endpoints, and authentication material
  • What it does not replace
  • Antivirus, firewall, web protection, and ASR policies still apply separately
  • For Windows, set the client configuration package type to Auto from connector when the Intune-Defender connection is active.
Slide 9

Investigate and respond to endpoint threats using Microsoft Defender

  • Investigate
  • Start with incident triage: severity, category, affected assets, and summary
  • Review related alerts, evidence, entities, timelines, and the attack story
  • Investigate suspicious processes, files, registry changes, command lines, and network connections
  • Open affected device pages for alerts, signed-in users, timeline, exposure, and actions
  • Respond
  • Isolate the device
  • Run an antivirus scan
  • Quarantine files
  • Use automated investigation and response (AIR)
  • Act from the device page or the Action center.
Slide 10

Monitor and triage incidents using Microsoft Defender XDR

  • Work the incident queue
  • Go to Investigation & response > Incidents & alerts > Incidents
  • Review severity, status, owner, impacted assets, category, source, and time
  • Filter for high-priority signals: privileged users, critical devices, active malware, or lateral movement
  • Five triage questions
  • What happened?
  • Which assets are affected?
  • Is the threat active?
  • What is the business impact?
  • What should happen next?

1. Intune toont een geslaagde onboardingpolicy. Wat controleer je aanvullend?

2. Waar onderzoek je samenhang tussen beveiligingsalerts?

Oorspronkelijke kennisvragen uit de cursus (Engels)

Unchanged question wording from the source decks, apart from typography. Answers and explanations, where present, come from the associated speaker notes. Treat them as course-source answers; validate current product behavior in Microsoft documentation.

Slide 11 · Question 1

Contoso wants to onboard its Windows 11 devices to Microsoft Defender with the least administrative effort. Devices are already enrolled in Microsoft Intune and the Intune-to-Defender service connection is enabled. Which approach should the endpoint administrator use?

  1. Download the onboarding package from the Microsoft Defender portal and deploy it as a custom configuration profile in Intune.
  2. Create an Endpoint detection and response policy in Intune for the Windows platform and set the client configuration package type to Auto from connector.
  3. Create a Microsoft Defender Antivirus policy in Intune and assign it to the Windows devices.
Answer and source explanation

Q1 - Correct: B. Create an Endpoint detection and response policy in Intune for the Windows platform and set the client configuration package type to Auto from connector. An EDR policy with Auto from connector uses the active Intune-to-Defender service connection to retrieve and deploy the current onboarding package to Windows devices. A. Manual onboarding packages are intended for environments where automatic retrieval isn't available; with the service connection enabled, an EDR policy can retrieve the package automatically. C. Antivirus policies configure Defender Antivirus settings; they don't onboard devices to Defender. Onboarding requires an EDR policy or onboarding package.

Slide 11 · Question 2

A security baseline assigned to a Windows device group enforces a setting that an existing endpoint security antivirus policy also configures with a different value. Where can the administrator see that two policies are setting the same value differently for the same device?

  1. In the Microsoft Defender portal under Settings > Endpoints > Advanced features.
  2. In the device's local Windows Security app on the endpoint itself.
  3. In the Microsoft Intune admin center on the policy status pages, which report policy conflicts when different policies apply different values for the same setting.
Answer and source explanation

Q2 - Correct: C. In the Microsoft Intune admin center on the policy status pages, which report policy conflicts when different policies apply different values for the same setting. Intune reports profile status, device status, and policy conflicts on the policy status pages when security baselines and endpoint security policies disagree on the same setting. A. The Defender portal Advanced features page configures tenant-level Defender capabilities and doesn't show Intune policy conflicts. B. The local Windows Security app shows the effective state of the device but doesn't surface Intune policy conflicts between baselines and endpoint security policies.

Slide 11 · Question 3

An analyst opens an incident in the Microsoft Defender portal and needs to understand what happened, which assets are affected, and whether the threat is still active. Which incident-page artifact best supports this triage step?

  1. The incident's correlated alerts, affected assets, evidence, and attack story view.
  2. The Microsoft Defender Antivirus exclusions list configured in Intune.
  3. The EDR Onboarding Status tab in the Intune admin center.
Answer and source explanation

Q3 - Correct: A. The incident's correlated alerts, affected assets, evidence, and attack story view. The incident view in the Microsoft Defender portal correlates alerts, affected assets, evidence, and the attack story so analysts can quickly answer triage questions about scope, impact, and activity. B. Antivirus exclusions show what Defender Antivirus skips during scans and don't describe the scope or activity of an active incident. C. The EDR Onboarding Status tab tracks whether devices are onboarded to Defender, not the details of an active incident.

Slide 12 · Question 1

An endpoint administrator wants to deploy attack surface reduction rules in audit mode first to measure impact before blocking. Which Intune workload should they use, and what's a prerequisite?

  1. Endpoint security > Attack surface reduction, with Microsoft Defender Antivirus as the primary antivirus on Windows devices.
  2. Devices > Configuration profiles > Administrative templates, with the device joined to an on-premises Active Directory domain.
  3. Endpoint security > Firewall, with a third-party antivirus configured as the primary antivirus on Windows devices.
Answer and source explanation

Q4 - Correct: A. Endpoint security > Attack surface reduction, with Microsoft Defender Antivirus as the primary antivirus on Windows devices. Attack surface reduction rules are deployed from the Endpoint security > Attack surface reduction node and require Windows devices that use Microsoft Defender Antivirus as the primary antivirus. B. Attack surface reduction rules are configured through the Endpoint security node in Intune, not administrative templates, and don't require an on-premises domain join. C. Firewall policies don't configure attack surface reduction rules, and a third-party primary antivirus prevents attack surface reduction from operating fully.

Slide 12 · Question 2

A high-severity incident in the Microsoft Defender portal involves a privileged account and signs of lateral movement. According to the recommended triage workflow, what should the analyst do next?

  1. Close the incident as informational because privileged accounts are expected to access multiple systems.
  2. Delete the impacted accounts and reimage every affected device immediately, without further triage.
  3. Assign an owner, add tags such as Privileged user and Needs escalation, summarize triage findings in comments, and notify the appropriate response team.
Answer and source explanation

Q5 - Correct: C. Assign an owner, add tags such as Privileged user and Needs escalation, summarize triage findings in comments, and notify the appropriate response team. The recommended escalation workflow is to assign an owner, tag the incident, summarize findings, update status, and notify the responsible team so the next responder has full context. A. High-severity activity involving privileged accounts and lateral movement should never be closed as informational without investigation; it warrants escalation. B. Destructive actions before triage can disrupt the business and destroy investigative evidence; triage and containment come before broad remediation.

Learning Path 04Module 02

Versleuteling en beveiligingsbeleid

Je configureert BitLocker, beschermt herstelsleutels en controleert de werkelijke versleutelingsstatus.

← Vorige moduleVolgende module →↑ Terug naar boven

Waarom is deze module belangrijk?

Versleuteling vermindert gegevensrisico bij verlies, maar onbereikbare herstelsleutels kunnen legitiem herstel blokkeren.

Leerdoelen

  • Uitleggen: versleuteling ontwerpen.
  • Uitleggen: beleid uitrollen.
  • Uitleggen: herstelsleutels beheren.
  • Uitleggen: status en afwijkingen.

01. Versleuteling ontwerpen

BitLocker beschermt ondersteunde Windows-volumes. Controleer editie, TPM, bestaande versleuteling en gewenste opstartbeveiliging. Maak onderscheid tussen beleid dat versleuteling vereist, configuratie die het inschakelt en rapportage die bevestigt dat het gebeurd is. Een toegewezen policy bewijst niet dat ieder volume beschermd is.

02. Beleid uitrollen

Gebruik geschikt disk-encryptionbeleid en test stille inschakeling in het gekozen scenario. Voorkom conflicten met andere BitLocker-profielen of lokale policies. Bepaal algoritme en herstelinstellingen voordat je uitrolt. Bestaande versleutelde volumes nemen niet noodzakelijk elke nieuwe instelling over zonder aanvullende handelingen.

03. Herstelsleutels beheren

Beveilig sleutelopslag en beperk wie sleutels mag opvragen. Controleer dat de recovery key op de verwachte locatie beschikbaar is vóór een herstelincident. Stem selfservice af op het organisatiebeleid. Registreer toegang en roteer waar passend een gebruikte sleutel. Deel sleutels nooit via onbeveiligde supportnotities.

04. Status en afwijkingen

Combineer Intune-encryptierapporten met compliance en aanvullende Defender-signalen. Onderzoek afwijkingen per apparaat, volume, TPM-status en beleidsbron. Exporteer alleen noodzakelijke informatie. Controleer bij herstel zowel de technische versleuteling als de veilige beschikbaarheid van het herstelmateriaal.

Wat moet je als beheerder onthouden?

  • Controleer werkelijke encryptie én sleutelopslag.
  • Recovery keys zijn gevoelige gegevens.
  • Compliance alleen schakelt BitLocker niet in.

Samenvatting

Controleer werkelijke encryptie én sleutelopslag. Recovery keys zijn gevoelige gegevens. Compliance alleen schakelt BitLocker niet in. Gebruik de hoofdstukken om keuzes te onderbouwen en controleer altijd de effectieve toestand naast de portalstatus.

Cursusverdieping per onderwerp (Engels) · 7 bronslides

Slide 15

Understand device encryption for compliance and security

  • The encryption chain : from drive to Zero Trust
  • 01 ENCRYPT · FDE
  • BitLocker / FileVault : drive is unreadable without the key; TPM ties it to the device
  • 02 ESCROW · key vault
  • Intune profile enables encryption silently; recovery key sent to Microsoft Entra ID
  • 03 EVALUATE · compliance
  • BitLocker / FileVault state checked at every check-in; flagged if disabled or corrupted
  • 04 ENFORCE · Zero Trust
  • Noncompliant signal revokes Microsoft 365 / VPN access until the drive is re-encrypted
  • Data at rest
  • Lock screen only protects the running OS
  • Pull the SSD or boot from USB and unencrypted data is exposed
  • Safe harbor
  • GDPR, HIPAA, PCI-DSS
  • An encrypted lost laptop is hardware loss, not a reportable breach
  • Key recovery
  • Admins pull keys from the Entra admin center
  • Users self-serve via the MyAccount portal
Slide 16

Configure BitLocker policies using Microsoft Intune

  • Three decisions take BitLocker from policy choice to a recoverable, encrypted fleet.
  • 1 · Policy type
  • Endpoint security > Disk encryption : focused
  • Settings catalog : bundle BitLocker with other Windows settings
  • Recommended: a separate BitLocker policy
  • Add Personal Data Encryption (PDE) for user files on Windows 11
  • 2 · Encryption
  • Encrypt OS and fixed drives; restrict removable
  • Cipher: XTS-AES 128 or 256, set at first encryption
  • Auth: TPM-only = silent · TPM + PIN = stronger
  • Escrow the recovery key to Microsoft Entra ID
  • 3 · Rollout
  • Assign to device groups for corporate fleets
  • Mode: silent if ready, standard with prompts
  • Monitor the encryption report + compliance status
  • Recover the key from the Entra device record
Slide 17

Manage BitLocker recovery keys and self-service options

  • Escrow once. Recover often. Rotate after every disclosure.
  • Phase
  • Action
  • What happens
  • State after
  • Build
  • Escrow
  • Save the recovery password to Microsoft Entra ID before BitLocker encrypts
  • Recoverable
  • Unlock
  • Self-serve
  • User signs into Company Portal (or MyAccount) and retrieves the key for their device
  • Unlocked
  • Assist
  • Helpdesk
  • Support verifies user, key ID, then reads the key from the Intune device record
  • Audited
  • Rotate
  • Refresh
  • Intune remote action or auto-rotation after every disclosure or recovery use
  • Renewed
Slide 18

Monitor BitLocker compliance and encryption status

  • Two views · different questions
  • Encryption report : reports the state
  • Devices > Monitor > Device encryption status
  • Readiness, encryption, TPM, and profile errors
  • Compliance view : decides access
  • Require BitLocker policy
  • Encryption state drives Compliant / Noncompliant : feeds Conditional Access
  • TIP Read in order: encryption status first · then compliance state : readiness is capability, status is current state.
  • Result
  • What it means
  • Ready & encrypted
  • Device is protected : move to a compliance state if access depends on it
  • Ready, not encrypted
  • Check whether the policy needs user action, a recent check-in, or encryption still in progress
  • Not ready
  • Review TPM state, Windows RE, OS support, and policy settings before reassignment
  • Encrypted, profile error
  • Drive encrypted with a method or protector that doesn't match policy : compare and align

Slide 20

Audit device encryption using Microsoft Defender for Endpoint

  • A hunting-focused view of encryption posture in Microsoft Defender : complements the Intune encryption report.
  • Layer 1 · Telemetry map

  • Inventory context : OSPlatform, LastSeenTime, AdditionalFields

  • Don't hard-code IDs : discover configurations first
  • Discovery: KB has_any ("BitLocker","Encryption")
  • Posture: join Assessment + KB + Inventory
  • Anomaly: group by device : failed controls or AssessmentAge > 7d
  • Layer 3 · Investigate
  • Treat query output as an audit queue, not a verdict
  • Correlate before you remediate
  • Prioritize by impact and cluster repeated failures
Slide 21

Audit device encryption : anomaly signals and workflow

  • Signal
  • What it means
  • Fail
  • Failed control : cross-check with the Intune encryption report and BitLocker state
  • Stale
  • Stale timestamp : check sensor health, onboarding, connectivity, last seen time
  • Impact
  • High-impact failed control : prioritize before lower-impact findings
  • Cluster
  • Repeated failures in one group : shared policy, hardware, or deployment cause
  • Audit workflow
  • → KB
  • Discover encryption configurations
  • → Assess
  • Query per-device applicability + compliance
  • → Context
  • Add inventory: OS, join, criticality
  • → Correlate
  • Validate in Intune: policy + recovery key

Kenniscontrole

Kies per vraag één antwoord. Je kunt opnieuw proberen; de score bepaalt je modulevoortgang niet.

1. Wat moet je vóór breed uitrollen van BitLocker aantonen?

2. Wat bewijst een encryptieprofiel met status toegewezen?

Oorspronkelijke kennisvragen uit de cursus (Engels)

Unchanged question wording from the source decks, apart from typography. Answers and explanations, where present, come from the associated speaker notes. Treat them as course-source answers; validate current product behavior in Microsoft documentation.

Slide 22 · Question 1

You want to roll BitLocker out to corporate Windows devices with minimum user friction. Devices have TPM 2.0 and meet readiness requirements. Which policy choice best matches a silent encryption goal?

  1. Endpoint security disk encryption policy that uses TPM-only startup authentication and stores recovery keys in Microsoft Entra ID.
  2. A settings catalog profile that requires TPM plus PIN at startup.
  3. An Endpoint security disk encryption policy that disables recovery key escrow so users can manage their own keys.
Answer and source explanation

Q1 - Correct: A. Endpoint security disk encryption policy that uses TPM-only startup authentication and stores recovery keys in Microsoft Entra ID. Correct. TPM-only startup authentication supports silent encryption because the user isn't prompted at boot, and storing keys in Microsoft Entra ID gives administrators a recoverable path before encryption begins. B. TPM plus PIN forces user interaction at every startup, which conflicts with a silent enablement goal even if it does increase startup protection. C. Disabling escrow leaves the organization without a managed recovery path and isn't required (or recommended) for silent encryption.

Slide 22 · Question 2

A user calls the helpdesk because their device entered BitLocker recovery. The technician retrieves and reads the recovery password to the user. What should the technician do immediately after recovery completes?

  1. Delete the device record from Intune to invalidate the disclosed key.
  2. Trigger the BitLocker key rotation remote action on the device in Intune.
  3. Email the recovery password to the user so they have it on file.
Answer and source explanation

Q2 - Correct: B. Trigger the BitLocker key rotation remote action on the device in Intune. Correct. Rotating the recovery password after disclosure ensures the value the technician just read can't be reused to unlock the device, and the new key is escrowed back to Microsoft Entra ID. A. Deleting the device record removes management and inventory data : it doesn't rotate or invalidate the BitLocker recovery password and creates many other problems. C. Emailing the recovery password broadens disclosure and creates a persistent copy outside the managed key store : the opposite of what should happen after a recovery event.

Slide 22 · Question 3

A security administrator wants only the support team to be able to read BitLocker recovery keys, without granting broader Intune or directory administration. Which approach follows the principle of least privilege?

  1. Assign each support technician the Global Administrator role.
  2. Have all support staff share a single Intune service account credential.
  3. Assign a built-in helpdesk role (or a custom role that grants only the BitLocker key read permission) scoped to the devices the team supports.
Answer and source explanation

Q3 - Correct: C. Assign a built-in helpdesk role (or a custom role that grants only the BitLocker key read permission) scoped to the devices the team supports. Correct. Built-in helpdesk roles or a custom role with just `microsoft.directory/bitlockerKeys/key/read` grant only what's needed, preserve individual audit trails, and can be scoped to a specific device set. A. Global Administrator grants far more access than is needed to read recovery keys and violates least-privilege guidance. B. Shared credentials defeat auditing : every key read should be traceable to an individual administrator.

Slide 23 · Question 1

You're building an encryption posture audit in Microsoft Defender. Which approach gives you a resilient, risk-prioritized view of which devices fail encryption-related controls?

  1. Hard-code a specific ConfigurationId in a hunting query and filter DeviceTvmSecureConfigurationAssessment against it.
  2. First query DeviceTvmSecureConfigurationAssessmentKB for BitLocker/Encryption-related configurations, then join those IDs to DeviceTvmSecureConfigurationAssessment filtered to IsApplicable == true, and prioritize by ConfigurationImpact and compliance.
  3. Rely solely on the Intune encryption report and skip Defender hunting entirely.
Answer and source explanation

Q4 - Correct: B. First query `DeviceTvmSecureConfigurationAssessmentKB` for BitLocker/Encryption-related configurations, then join those IDs to `DeviceTvmSecureConfigurationAssessment` filtered to `IsApplicable == true`, and prioritize by `ConfigurationImpact` and compliance. Correct. The two-step pattern discovers current encryption-related configurations from the knowledge base and joins them to per-device assessment results, producing a risk-prioritized view that survives configuration changes. A. Hard-coded IDs break when configuration identifiers change or new encryption controls are added. The recommended pattern discovers IDs from the knowledge base first. C. The Intune encryption report validates policy assignment and BitLocker state, but it doesn't replace the security-operations hunting view in Microsoft Defender that surfaces failed controls and stale telemetry.

Slide 23 · Question 2

Which Intune configuration combination ensures a device can't begin BitLocker encryption until a recoverable key exists in your tenant?

  1. Enable silent encryption and disable recovery key escrow to Microsoft Entra ID.
  2. Save BitLocker recovery information to Microsoft Entra ID and require that recovery information is stored before BitLocker starts.
  3. Configure TPM plus PIN startup authentication and rely on the user to record the PIN.
Answer and source explanation

Q5 - Correct: B. Save BitLocker recovery information to Microsoft Entra ID and require that recovery information is stored before BitLocker starts. Correct. Combining recovery information escrow to Microsoft Entra ID with the requirement that storage succeeds before encryption begins guarantees a recoverable key exists in the tenant the moment encryption starts. A. Disabling escrow removes the recovery path entirely : devices might encrypt, but you'd have no managed key if recovery is needed. C. Startup authentication protects the boot path but doesn't store a recoverable key. A PIN that only the user knows isn't a managed recovery method.

Learning Path 04Module 03

Geavanceerde dreigingsbescherming en Zero Trust

Je combineert preventie, cloudappinzichten en voortdurende verificatie van toegang.

← Vorige moduleVolgende module →↑ Terug naar boven

Waarom is deze module belangrijk?

Beveiliging vraagt meerdere samenwerkende lagen omdat een apparaat of sessie later alsnog risicovol kan worden.

Leerdoelen

  • Uitleggen: taken verdelen.
  • Uitleggen: attack Surface Reduction.
  • Uitleggen: cloudapps onderzoeken.
  • Uitleggen: zero Trust toepassen.

01. Taken verdelen

Intune levert configuratie en beleidsdistributie. Defender for Endpoint detecteert endpointdreigingen en ondersteunt respons. Defender for Cloud Apps brengt cloudappgebruik en gerelateerde risico’s in beeld. Bepaal voor iedere laag wie signalen opvolgt en welke gegevens worden uitgewisseld.

02. Attack Surface Reduction

ASR-regels beperken gedrag dat aanvallen vaak misbruiken. Begin waar passend met audit om de impact op legitieme processen te begrijpen. Onderzoek gebeurtenissen en gebruik gerichte uitzonderingen. Schakel pas na validatie over naar blokkering. Eén brede uitzondering kan het effect van meerdere regels ondermijnen.

03. Cloudapps onderzoeken

Cloudappinzichten kunnen ongewenste diensten en risicovol gedrag helpen ontdekken. Beoordeel datastromen, gebruikspatronen en bedrijfsbehoefte voordat je een dienst beperkt. Koppel ontdekking aan beleid en gebruikerscommunicatie. Een gevonden cloudapp is niet automatisch kwaadaardig; context en classificatie bepalen de opvolging.

04. Zero Trust toepassen

Verifieer expliciet, geef minimale rechten en ga uit van mogelijke compromittering. Combineer identiteit, apparaatcompliance en risicosignalen voor toegangsbeslissingen. Test Conditional Access eerst gecontroleerd en houd hersteltoegang beschikbaar. Evalueer beleid opnieuw na wijzigingen in gebruikers, apparaten of dreigingen.

Wat moet je als beheerder onthouden?

  • Test ASR-impact voordat je breed blokkeert.
  • Combineer preventie met detectie en respons.
  • Zero Trust vraagt herhaalde verificatie en minimale rechten.

Samenvatting

Test ASR-impact voordat je breed blokkeert. Combineer preventie met detectie en respons. Zero Trust vraagt herhaalde verificatie en minimale rechten. Gebruik de hoofdstukken om keuzes te onderbouwen en controleer altijd de effectieve toestand naast de portalstatus.

Cursusverdieping per onderwerp (Engels) · 5 bronslides

Slide 26

Understand advanced threat protection strategies for endpoint environments

  • A defense lifecycle across the endpoint estate : each stage feeds the next.
  • Prevention
  • Harden with baselines, Defender Antivirus, firewall, network protection
  • ASR rules, vulnerability remediation, app restrictions
  • Detection
  • Defender for Endpoint telemetry, cloud protection, behavioral analytics
  • Threat intelligence, EDR, advanced hunting, vulnerability insights
  • Response
  • Isolate devices, run AV scans, collect investigation packages
  • Quarantine files, AIR, and Microsoft Defender XDR
  • Recovery & improvement
  • Restore trusted state, remediate vulnerabilities, update policies
  • Review evidence and feed lessons learned back into controls
Slide 27

Advanced threat protection : how Intune and Defender divide the work

  • Microsoft Intune : policy & management
  • Onboard devices and deploy endpoint security policies
  • Apply security baselines and configure compliance
  • Support remediation through policy changes and security tasks
  • Defender for Endpoint : detection & response
  • Monitor endpoint activity and evaluate device risk
  • Identify vulnerabilities and investigate incidents
  • Trigger response actions in Microsoft Defender XDR
  • Together: Intune sets and enforces the guardrails; Defender watches, scores risk, and drives the response.
Slide 28

Discover and monitor cloud apps using Microsoft Defender for Cloud Apps

  • Use Microsoft Defender for Cloud Apps to identify shadow IT, assess risk, and monitor cloud app behavior.
  • Discover & assess
  • Discovers cloud services used by users and devices
  • Highlights shadow IT: traffic volume, users, IPs, devices, and risk scores
  • Sources: uploaded traffic logs, continuous log collection, or Defender for Endpoint signals
  • Cloud App Catalog risk factors: compliance, legal, security, privacy, app characteristics
  • Govern
  • Tag apps as Sanctioned or Unsanctioned to support monitoring and enforcement
  • Investigate before blocking: identify business need, users, uploads, OAuth permissions, and safer alternatives
Slide 29

Configure Attack Surface Reduction (ASR) rules using Microsoft Intune

  • Common ASR rules
  • Block Office apps from creating child processes
  • Block executable content from email and webmail
  • Block credential stealing from LSASS
  • Block scripts that launch downloaded executable content
  • Block abuse of vulnerable signed drivers or copied system tools
  • Deploy safely
  • Configure from Endpoint security > Attack surface reduction
  • Start in Audit mode on a pilot group
  • Review reported events and add exclusions
  • Switch to Block mode to enforce across the fleet
  • Pilot in Audit mode before switching to Block.
Slide 30

Apply Zero Trust principles for endpoint protection

  • Continuously verify identity, device health, compliance, and risk before allowing access.
  • Verify explicitly
  • Check user identity, device health, compliance state, risk signals, and resource sensitivity before access
  • Use least privilege
  • Grant only the access users and devices need, for only as long as they need it
  • Assume breach
  • Design controls as if a user account, device, or network segment may already be compromised
  • Endpoint enforcement flow
  • Defender
  • Detects active malware
  • Risk → High
  • Device risk becomes High
  • Intune
  • Marks the device noncompliant
  • Conditional Access
  • Blocks access until remediation

Kenniscontrole

Kies per vraag één antwoord. Je kunt opnieuw proberen; de score bepaalt je modulevoortgang niet.

1. Waarom begin je een ASR-uitrol vaak met audit?

2. Welke uitspraak past bij Zero Trust?

Oorspronkelijke kennisvragen uit de cursus (Engels)

Unchanged question wording from the source decks, apart from typography. Answers and explanations, where present, come from the associated speaker notes. Treat them as course-source answers; validate current product behavior in Microsoft documentation.

Slide 31 · Question 1

A security team wants to roll out an Attack Surface Reduction rule that blocks Office apps from creating child processes, but is concerned a line-of-business macro might break. What's the recommended first step?

  1. Deploy the ASR rule in Block mode to a pilot group and review user-reported issues.
  2. Configure the ASR rule in Audit mode for a pilot group and review reported events before enforcing it.
  3. Add a permanent exclusion for the LOB app and deploy the rule in Block mode to all devices immediately.
Answer and source explanation

Q1 - Correct: B. Configure the ASR rule in Audit mode for a pilot group and review reported events before enforcing it. Audit mode shows what the rule would block without affecting users, so admins can identify needed exclusions before moving to Block. A. Starting in Block mode risks disrupting line-of-business apps before impact is known. C. Broad exclusions and immediate Block mode bypass the recommended pilot-and-audit deployment pattern.

Slide 31 · Question 2

Users are connecting to cloud apps from remote networks that don't route through the corporate proxy. Which Microsoft Defender data source best provides ongoing discovery for those endpoints?

  1. Snapshot reports from manually uploaded firewall logs.
  2. Defender's endpoint integration for cloud discovery.
  3. A continuous report from the on-premises secure web gateway.
Answer and source explanation

Q2 - Correct: B. Defender's endpoint integration for cloud discovery. Defender's endpoint integration discovers cloud app usage directly from managed endpoints, including remote devices not behind the corporate network. A. Snapshot reports give a point-in-time view from logs you upload manually : they don't cover remote endpoints continuously. C. An on-premises SWG only sees traffic that traverses it, missing remote users.

Slide 31 · Question 3

An admin wants to repeatedly detect and fix a misconfigured registry value on managed Windows devices without manual intervention. Which Microsoft Intune capability fits this requirement?

  1. A proactive remediation script package with detection and remediation scripts.
  2. An Attack Surface Reduction rule in Audit mode.
  3. A device compliance policy that marks the device noncompliant.
Answer and source explanation

Q3 - Correct: A. A proactive remediation script package with detection and remediation scripts. Proactive remediations run a detection script on a schedule and run the remediation script automatically when the detection finds the issue. B. ASR rules block behaviors associated with attacks; they don't detect or fix arbitrary registry values. C. Compliance policies report on state but don't change device configuration to remediate the issue.

Learning Path 04Module 04

Compliance afdwingen en beveiligingsproblemen herstellen

Je bouwt de keten van apparaatvereisten naar toegangsbeslissing en aantoonbaar herstel.

← Vorige moduleVolgende module →↑ Terug naar boven

Waarom is deze module belangrijk?

Een onduidelijke compliance-aanpak kan gebruikers blokkeren zonder hun apparaat werkelijk veiliger te maken.

Leerdoelen

  • Uitleggen: eisen en signalen.
  • Uitleggen: scope en gevolgen.
  • Uitleggen: conditional Access.
  • Uitleggen: herstellen en controleren.

01. Eisen en signalen

Compliancebeleid beoordeelt platformafhankelijke eisen zoals OS-versie, versleuteling en dreigingsniveau. Kies eisen die je kunt uitleggen en ondersteunen. Controleer de tenantinstelling voor apparaten zonder toegewezen compliancebeleid. Onderscheid noncompliant, niet geëvalueerd en verouderde status in je analyse.

02. Scope en gevolgen

Gebruik groepen en ondersteunde filters om beleid bij de juiste apparaten te laten landen. Configureer passende actions for noncompliance, zoals melding en een eventuele hersteltermijn. Een grace period is een bewuste risicoafweging. Laat gebruikers weten welke instelling faalt en hoe zij kunnen herstellen.

03. Conditional Access

Conditional Access kan compliant devices vereisen voor toegang tot resources. Intune levert het signaal; Entra voert de toegangsbeslissing uit. Test beleid met beperkte doelgroepen en report-only waar toepasbaar. Beheer noodtoegang zorgvuldig en voorkom dat een brede wijziging alle beheerkanalen blokkeert.

04. Herstellen en controleren

Gebruik configuratie, endpointsecurity of een passend herstelproces om de foutieve toestand te corrigeren. Synchroniseer en controleer de nieuwe compliance-evaluatie en aanmeldlogs. Een uitzondering verwijdert de oorzaak niet. Gebruik rapporten om terugkerende fouten, ontbrekende toewijzingen en problematische doelgroepen structureel aan te pakken.

Wat moet je als beheerder onthouden?

  • Compliance meet; herstel verandert de toestand.
  • Intune en Entra hebben verschillende rollen in de toegangsketen.
  • Controleer ook apparaten zonder toegewezen beleid.

Samenvatting

Compliance meet; herstel verandert de toestand. Intune en Entra hebben verschillende rollen in de toegangsketen. Controleer ook apparaten zonder toegewezen beleid. Gebruik de hoofdstukken om keuzes te onderbouwen en controleer altijd de effectieve toestand naast de portalstatus.

Cursusverdieping per onderwerp (Engels) · 10 bronslides

Original course content. Text order follows the source presentation; visual tables and diagrams are represented as text.

Slide 34

Understand compliance policies and risk-based enforcement

  • Compliance evaluation runs in layers : static checks, live risk, then graduated escalation.
  • Layer 1 · Static baseline
  • Local config checks : fail one and Intune flags Non-compliant
  • Device health : Windows Health Attestation; block jailbroken iOS / rooted Android
  • OS versioning : set min/max OS to close zero-day windows fast
  • System security : PIN complexity, encryption, firewall, AV up to date
  • Layer 2 · Dynamic risk
  • Static checks miss live threats : risk feeds back to Intune
  • Signal source : Defender for Endpoint · Mobile Threat Defense partners
  • Machine risk score : Clear / Low / Medium / High, real time
  • Rule : require device at or under risk score Low
  • Layer 3 · Escalation
  • Don't go straight to a block : nudge first
  • Day 0 : mark noncompliant; CA can block immediately
  • Day 0-1 : email the user (CC manager) with the fix link
  • Day 1 : push to Company Portal · Day 30+ : retire + wipe
Slide 35

Compliance and Conditional Access : the Zero Trust chain

  • Intune evaluates · Entra Conditional Access enforces.
  • Defender
  • User clicks a phishing link : risk score goes High
  • Intune
  • Evaluates compliance : marks the device Non-compliant
  • Conditional Access
  • Sees the Non-compliant tag : blocks Teams / Microsoft 365
  • Remediation
  • Email + push to user : run an AV scan to clear
  • The device state flows from Defender to Intune to Conditional Access automatically : remediation clears it and restores access.
Slide 36

Create compliance policies for supported platforms

  • Two policies · different jobs
  • Configuration profile : forces the state
  • Pushes settings to the device OS
  • Turns BitLocker on, sets XTS-AES 256
  • Compliance policy : verifies the state
  • Asks the device: is BitLocker actually on right now?
  • Marks it Compliant or Noncompliant
  • TIP Name them apart: Win10-Config-BitLocker · Win10-Comp-BitLockerRequired
  • Platform
  • What Intune can check
  • Windows 10+
  • Hardware checks, Secure Boot, TPM health, code integrity, Defender Firewall & AV, Defender for Endpoint risk score
  • iOS / iPadOS
  • Apple hardware-backed checks, jailbreak status, Secure Enclave passcode, OS version currency
  • Android Enterprise
  • Work profile (BYOD) corp container; Fully Managed whole device, Play Protect attestation, root status
  • macOS
  • FileVault encryption, System Integrity Protection (SIP), Gatekeeper, password and OS version rules
Slide 37

Actions for noncompliance : graduated enforcement

  • Build a grace period : don't block users on day zero.
  • When
  • Action
  • What it does
  • Day 0
  • Mark noncompliant
  • Status flows to Microsoft Entra ID
  • Day 1
  • Email the user
  • Auto-remediation gets a shot first
  • Day 3-7
  • Push to Company Portal
  • Pop-up on the mobile device
  • Day 7-14
  • Block via CA · lock
  • Force action when nudges fail
  • Day 30+
  • Retire the device
  • Wipe corporate data : last resort
Slide 38

Assign and scope compliance policies using groups and filters

  • The scope funnel : from everyone to exactly the right devices.
  • 01 Cast · Groups
  • Entra user or device group : who or what carries the policy
  • 02 Automate · Dynamic
  • Rule-based: user.department, device.deviceOwnership : self-maintaining
  • 03 Narrow · Filters
  • Include / exclude, evaluated at every check-in : no group bloat
  • 04 Land · Applied
  • Right devices · right people · no collateral damage
  • User vs device
  • A user group follows the person
  • A device group pins to the hardware
  • Automation
  • Dynamic rules cut admin toil
  • Governance over attribute editing is critical
  • Validation
  • Sync · filter eval · compliance eval
  • Status reported back to Intune
Slide 39

Configure actions for noncompliant devices

  • PowerShell fix script silently corrects the value
  • 3 · Targeted

1. Een apparaat faalt op encryptie. Wat lost de oorzaak op?

2. Welke dienst voert Conditional Access uit?

Oorspronkelijke kennisvragen uit de cursus (Engels)

Unchanged question wording from the source decks, apart from typography. Answers and explanations, where present, come from the associated speaker notes. Treat them as course-source answers; validate current product behavior in Microsoft documentation.

Slide 44 · Question 1

A Windows compliance policy requires BitLocker to be enabled, but several devices report as noncompliant even though IT believes encryption was configured. Which Intune feature should an administrator deploy alongside the compliance policy to *enforce* the encryption setting and allow devices to self-heal?

  1. A device configuration profile (or Endpoint Security disk encryption policy) that actively configures BitLocker on the device.
  2. A second compliance policy that also checks for BitLocker, scoped to the same group.
  3. A Microsoft Entra Conditional Access policy that blocks noncompliant devices.
Answer and source explanation

Q1 - Correct: A. A device configuration profile (or Endpoint Security disk encryption policy) that actively configures BitLocker on the device. Compliance policies only verify state. A configuration profile or Endpoint Security disk encryption policy is required to actively enforce BitLocker so the device can self-heal back into compliance. B. Adding another compliance policy still only checks state: it never configures BitLocker on the device, so the noncompliance persists. C. Conditional Access enforces *access* based on compliance status, but it doesn't configure BitLocker or remediate the underlying setting.

Slide 44 · Question 2

An administrator wants to apply a strict compliance policy to all Windows devices *except* a small set of executive laptops, without creating a new Microsoft Entra group for the exclusions. Which Intune capability best fits this requirement?

  1. An assignment filter that evaluates device properties (such as device name or OS) at assignment time.
  2. A dynamic device group with a membership rule for every executive laptop.
  3. Scope tags applied to the compliance policy.
Answer and source explanation

Q2 - Correct: A. An assignment filter that evaluates device properties (such as device name or OS) at assignment time. Assignment filters let you include or exclude devices based on properties like device name, OS version, or manufacturer without creating additional Microsoft Entra groups. B. Dynamic groups can work, but the scenario explicitly asks to avoid creating a new group; assignment filters address this exact use case. C. Scope tags control which Intune admins can see or edit a policy; they don't change which devices the policy targets.

Slide 44 · Question 3

After deploying a new compliance policy, an administrator configures the action Mark device noncompliant with a schedule of 3 days. While the schedule is active, how does Microsoft Entra ID evaluate a device that has just failed an evaluation?

  1. The device is reported as In grace period, which is still a noncompliant state, and standard Conditional Access policies requiring compliance will block access by default.
  2. The device is reported as Compliant until the 3 days expire, allowing uninterrupted access.
  3. The device is immediately retired from Intune to force re-enrollment.
Answer and source explanation

Q3 - Correct: A. The device is reported as **In grace period**, which is still a noncompliant state, and standard Conditional Access policies requiring compliance will block access by default. Grace period is technically a noncompliant state. Unless a Conditional Access policy explicitly allows the **Require device to be in grace period** control, access is blocked. B. Intune never falsely reports a device as Compliant during a grace period: the device is still considered noncompliant. C. Retire is a separate, manually configured action and isn't triggered by a standard grace-period schedule.

Slide 45 · Question 1

A noncompliant Windows device is failing because a custom registry key required by a line-of-business application is missing. Which Intune feature is best suited to automatically detect and fix this issue?

  1. Intune Remediations using a detection script and a remediation PowerShell script.
  2. An additional compliance policy that checks the registry key.
  3. A Microsoft Entra Conditional Access policy targeting the device.
Answer and source explanation

Q4 - Correct: A. Intune Remediations using a detection script and a remediation PowerShell script. Intune Remediations (formerly Proactive Remediations) run detection and remediation scripts on a schedule, making them ideal for custom, scripted fixes such as missing registry keys. B. A compliance policy can only check state: it can't create the missing registry key. C. Conditional Access controls access to resources; it doesn't modify the device or fix missing registry keys.

Slide 45 · Question 2

An auditor asks for evidence that all corporate Windows devices have BitLocker enabled and Microsoft Defender Antivirus running, with point-in-time data the auditor can review offline. Which Microsoft Intune capability provides this evidence most efficiently?

  1. Generate and export compliance reports from Reports > Device compliance in the Microsoft Intune admin center.
  2. Have the auditor sign in to the Microsoft Intune admin center and browse each device individually.
  3. Open a Microsoft Entra sign-in log filtered by device name.
Answer and source explanation

Q5 - Correct: A. Generate and export compliance reports from Reports > Device compliance in the Microsoft Intune admin center. The compliance monitoring dashboards and exportable reports provide tenant-wide point-in-time evidence of device health, suitable for audit packages. B. Per-device browsing doesn't scale, isn't exportable, and typically violates least-privilege auditing practices. C. Microsoft Entra sign-in logs show authentication events, not the configured state of BitLocker or Defender on each device.

Learning Path 04Module 05

Mobiele toegang beveiligen met Microsoft Tunnel

Je beschrijft een gecontroleerde VPN-route voor mobiele apps naar interne resources.

← Vorige moduleVolgende module →↑ Terug naar boven

Waarom is deze module belangrijk?

Mobiele gebruikers hebben soms interne diensten nodig zonder die rechtstreeks op internet beschikbaar te maken.

Leerdoelen

  • Uitleggen: gateway en netwerkroute.
  • Uitleggen: clientconfiguratie en per-app VPN.
  • Uitleggen: tunnel for MAM.
  • Uitleggen: operationele bewaking.

01. Gateway en netwerkroute

Microsoft Tunnel gebruikt een gateway in een ondersteunde Linux-omgeving voor toegang tot interne resources. Controleer containerplatform, netwerkpoorten, certificaten, DNS en bereikbaarheid. Ontwerp beschikbaarheid en onderhoud voordat je cliënten uitrolt. Een werkende internetverbinding bewijst niet dat interne naamomzetting goed functioneert.

02. Clientconfiguratie en per-app VPN

Wijs platformgeschikte VPN-configuratie toe en bepaal welke apps de tunnel gebruiken. Per-app VPN beperkt de route tot geselecteerd zakelijk verkeer. Controleer de vereiste clientcomponenten voor het platform. Test zowel verbinding als een echte interne resource, inclusief DNS en certificaatvertrouwen.

03. Tunnel for MAM

Tunnel for MAM ondersteunt specifieke scenario’s met ondersteunde apps zonder volledige apparaatinschrijving. App protection, appconfiguratie, platformondersteuning en aanvullende licenties spelen samen. Neem niet aan dat ieder willekeurig programma de route kan gebruiken. Beoordeel de actuele ondersteuningsmatrix voor de doelgroep.

04. Operationele bewaking

Monitor gatewaystatus, verbindingen en certificaatverval. Bij fouten onderzoek je authenticatie, netwerkroute en interne resource afzonderlijk. Controleer logs en wijzigingen in firewalls of DNS. Plan vernieuwing en platformupdates zodat een verlopen certificaat of niet-ondersteunde host de toegang niet onverwacht onderbreekt.

Wat moet je als beheerder onthouden?

  • Controleer de volledige route inclusief interne DNS.
  • Per-app VPN begrenst welke apps de tunnel gebruiken.
  • Tunnel for MAM heeft specifieke app- en licentievoorwaarden.

Samenvatting

Controleer de volledige route inclusief interne DNS. Per-app VPN begrenst welke apps de tunnel gebruiken. Tunnel for MAM heeft specifieke app- en licentievoorwaarden. Gebruik de hoofdstukken om keuzes te onderbouwen en controleer altijd de effectieve toestand naast de portalstatus.

Cursusverdieping per onderwerp (Engels) · 4 bronslides

Configure Microsoft Tunnel Gateway

  • Component

  • Setup

  • Foundation set

  • Install
  • Run `mstunnel-setup` on Linux · add a TLS cert · join the site
  • Server online
  • Step 3
  • Client

  • Tunnel-ready
  • Step 4

  • Per-platform VPN profile points devices at your Tunnel site

Slide 49

Extend Tunnel support to MAM devices

  • Bring the same VPN gateway to unenrolled BYOD phones : no device management required.
  • Layer 1 · Concept
  • License : Intune Plan 2 or the Microsoft Intune Suite add-on
  • Scope : personal Android and iOS phones; personal data stays separate
  • Identity : Microsoft Entra sign-in; Conditional Access still applies
  • Layer 2 · Platforms
  • Android 10.0+ : per-app or device-wide VPN, delivered via Defender
  • iOS 17.0+ : per-app VPN only (latest + two previous)
  • Delivery : Defender app on Android · Tunnel for MAM SDK on iOS
  • Layer 3 · Policy stack

  • Edge config : StrictTunnelMode, work/personal identity switching

Slide 50

Tunnel for MAM : the BYOD connection flow

  • User signs into Edge · Tunnel connects · Conditional Access enforces.
  • User
  • Opens Edge or an LOB app; signs in with the work account
  • Tunnel
  • VPN auto-connects, scoped to managed apps
  • Conditional Access
  • Evaluates the request
  • Resource
  • On-prem app or file share reached without enrollment
  • Three policies wire together so the VPN starts the moment a managed app launches : no full device enrollment needed.
Slide 51

Monitor and troubleshoot Tunnel connections

  • Connect fail
  • Symptom : devices can't open; /dev/net/tun denied
  • Cause : host rebooted before Tunnel restarted
  • Fix : sudo mst-cli server restart
  • Cron the restart if the Linux host reboots often
  • Agent drift
  • Symptom : server shows offline but devices still connect
  • Cause : Tunnel agent lost its Intune enrollment
  • Fix : mst-cli uninstall then mstunnel-setup
  • Apply agent and server updates promptly
  • Cert expiry
  • Symptom : TLS certificate metric shows Warning
  • Prep : copy a new cert with a valid SAN to the server
  • Fix : mst-cli import_cert then server restart
  • Use import_cert delay <minutes> to stage the cutover
  • Monitor: Health status & Trends dashboard · mst-cli server status / server show · journalctl -t ocserv · send verbose logs to Support (Incident ID)

1. De VPN verbindt, maar een interne hostnaam werkt niet. Wat onderzoek je?

Oorspronkelijke kennisvragen uit de cursus (Engels)

Unchanged question wording from the source decks, apart from typography. Answers and explanations, where present, come from the associated speaker notes. Treat them as course-source answers; validate current product behavior in Microsoft documentation.

Slide 52 · Question 1

You're preparing the TLS certificate for a new Microsoft Tunnel Gateway server that devices reach using the FQDN tunnel.contoso.com. What requirement must the certificate meet?

  1. It must be a self-signed certificate generated by the mst-cli tool on the Linux host.
  2. The Subject Alternative Name (SAN) must include the IP address or FQDN that devices use to reach the server.
  3. The certificate must be issued exclusively by the Microsoft Intune root CA.
Answer and source explanation

Q1 - Correct: B. The Subject Alternative Name (SAN) must include the IP address or FQDN that devices use to reach the server. Correct. The certificate copied to `/etc/mstunnel/private/site.pfx` (or the matching PEM files) must include the public IP or FQDN devices connect to in its SAN, or the TLS handshake fails. A. Self-signed certificates aren't supported for Tunnel server identity. The server presents a TLS certificate that clients validate, so it needs a chain devices trust. C. Any certificate from a CA the connecting devices trust is acceptable : there's no requirement that it comes from a Microsoft-issued CA.

Slide 52 · Question 2

Your organization wants unenrolled Android personal devices to use Microsoft Tunnel for accessing on-premises resources from Microsoft Edge. Which prerequisite must be in place?

  1. The devices must first be fully enrolled in Intune as personally owned work-profile devices.
  2. An Intune Plan 2 license (or Intune Suite add-on) covering the users, plus app configuration and app protection policies on Edge that enable Tunnel on app launch.
  3. Each user must install OpenSSH on their device to authenticate to the Tunnel gateway.
Answer and source explanation

Q2 - Correct: B. An Intune Plan 2 license (or Intune Suite add-on) covering the users, plus app configuration and app protection policies on Edge that enable Tunnel on app launch. Correct. Tunnel for MAM is licensed under Intune Plan 2 / Intune Suite, and on Android it requires the Microsoft Defender app configuration policy, the Edge app config policy with the StrictTunnelMode and TunnelAvailable keys, and an Edge app protection policy that starts the Tunnel connection on app launch. A. Tunnel for MAM is specifically for unenrolled devices : that's the scenario it solves. Requiring enrollment would defeat the purpose. C. Tunnel authenticates users through Microsoft Entra ID : SSH isn't part of the connection flow on client devices.

Slide 52 · Question 3

You're rolling out Microsoft Tunnel for MAM on iOS for a line-of-business application. What scope of VPN coverage can you provide on this platform?

  1. Device-wide VPN that routes all iOS traffic through the Tunnel gateway.
  2. Per-app VPN only, scoped to the LOB app (and other apps such as Edge) that integrate the Tunnel for MAM iOS SDK.
  3. Always-on VPN that connects automatically at device boot for any signed-in user.
Answer and source explanation

Q3 - Correct: B. Per-app VPN only, scoped to the LOB app (and other apps such as Edge) that integrate the Tunnel for MAM iOS SDK. Correct. On iOS, Tunnel for MAM is per-app only. The VPN applies to apps that integrate the SDK and have the right app configuration / app protection policies assigned. A. Tunnel for MAM on iOS doesn't support device-wide VPN : coverage is limited to apps that integrate the Tunnel for MAM iOS SDK. C. Always-on VPN requires device enrollment and isn't part of the MAM-only Tunnel experience on iOS.

Slide 53 · Question 1

A Tunnel Gateway server shows as offline in the admin center's Health status view, but devices are still connecting successfully through it. Which corrective action is recommended?

  1. Run mst-cli server restart and consider the issue resolved if devices stay connected.
  2. Reinstall Microsoft Tunnel (sudo mst-cli uninstall followed by sudo ./mstunnel-setup) so the agent re-registers with Intune.
  3. Delete the site in the Intune admin center and recreate it from scratch.
Answer and source explanation

Q4 - Correct: B. Reinstall Microsoft Tunnel (`sudo mst-cli uninstall` followed by `sudo ./mstunnel-setup`) so the agent re-registers with Intune. Correct. The known cause is that the Tunnel agent lost its enrollment registration with Intune while the server component kept working. Reinstalling re-enrolls the agent and restores the admin center health view. A. Restarting the server doesn't re-enroll the Tunnel agent with Intune, which is the root cause when the server reports healthy locally but Intune shows it offline. C. Deleting the site impacts every device assigned to it and doesn't fix an agent registration problem on a single server.

Slide 53 · Question 2

Your network team won't let Tunnel use any UDP traffic to the gateway because of a firewall constraint. What configuration change supports this requirement?

  1. Disable the mstunnel-agent service on the Linux host.
  2. In the server configuration, enable the Disable UDP connections option (supported when devices use the Microsoft Defender Tunnel client).
  3. Add 0.0.0.0/0 to the split-tunneling exclude list to force TCP routing.
Answer and source explanation

Q5 - Correct: B. In the server configuration, enable the **Disable UDP connections** option (supported when devices use the Microsoft Defender Tunnel client). Correct. The server configuration includes a **Disable UDP connections** toggle intended for TCP-only environments where the Microsoft Defender Tunnel client is used. A. Stopping the agent breaks management and reporting for the Tunnel server : it isn't how you switch the client transport to TCP-only. C. Split tunneling routes traffic by destination, not by transport protocol. Also, `0.0.0.0` isn't supported in split-tunneling rules.

Learning Path 04Module 06

Microsoft Cloud PKI implementeren

Je beheert certificaatuitgifte voor apparaten met een cloudgebaseerde certificaatautoriteit.

← Vorige moduleVolgende module →↑ Terug naar boven

Waarom is deze module belangrijk?

Certificaatgebaseerde toegang vereist niet alleen uitgifte, maar ook vertrouwen, vernieuwing en intrekking.

Leerdoelen

  • Uitleggen: pKI en CA-rollen.
  • Uitleggen: vertrouwen en profielen.
  • Uitleggen: uitgifte, renewal en revocation.
  • Uitleggen: gezondheid bewaken.

01. PKI en CA-rollen

Een root CA vormt een vertrouwensanker; een issuing CA geeft eindcertificaten uit binnen de gekozen hiërarchie. Microsoft Cloud PKI neemt delen van de CA-infrastructuur uit handen. Beoordeel licentie en ondersteunde scenario’s. Een cloud-CA maakt de noodzaak van correct vertrouwen bij Wi-Fi-, VPN- of andere diensten niet ongedaan.

02. Vertrouwen en profielen

Distribueer vertrouwde CA-certificaten en passende certificaatprofielen naar de juiste doelgroep. Stel onderwerp, gebruiksdoel en geldigheidsduur af op de authenticatiedienst. Bij SCEP-gebruik moeten profielinstellingen en de gekozen issuing CA op elkaar aansluiten. Test met een beperkt aantal apparaten en een echte resource.

03. Uitgifte, renewal en revocation

Certificaten hebben een levenscyclus. Controleer automatische vernieuwing ruim vóór verloop en leg intrekking vast bij verlies, compromittering of uitdienstname. De ontvangende dienst moet de relevante vertrouwens- en intrekkingsinformatie kunnen gebruiken. Een nieuw certificaat helpt niet als de resource de uitgevende keten niet vertrouwt.

04. Gezondheid bewaken

Monitor uitgiftefouten, verlopen certificaten en mislukte authenticatie. Onderzoek profieltoewijzing, apparaatbereikbaarheid en certificaatinhoud. Beperk CA-beheerrechten en documenteer wijzigingen. Plan tijdig de lifecycle van de CA zelf en controleer dat afhankelijkheden na vernieuwing blijven werken.

Wat moet je als beheerder onthouden?

  • Uitgifte en vertrouwen zijn verschillende voorwaarden.
  • Beheer intrekking en vernieuwing expliciet.
  • Test certificaten tegen de werkelijke authenticatiedienst.

Samenvatting

Uitgifte en vertrouwen zijn verschillende voorwaarden. Beheer intrekking en vernieuwing expliciet. Test certificaten tegen de werkelijke authenticatiedienst. Gebruik de hoofdstukken om keuzes te onderbouwen en controleer altijd de effectieve toestand naast de portalstatus.

Cursusverdieping per onderwerp (Engels) · 4 bronslides

Original course content. Text order follows the source presentation; visual tables and diagrams are represented as text.

Slide 56

Set up cloud-based PKI in Microsoft Intune

  • Three steps stand up a two-tier cloud PKI in Intune : hierarchy, trust, then SCEP.
  • 1 · Hierarchy
  • Tenant administration > Cloud PKI > Create
  • Two-tier hierarchy · keys in Azure Managed HSM
  • 2 · Trust
  • Devices > Configuration > Trusted certificate
  • One profile per CA : root first, then issuing
  • 3 · SCEP
  • Devices > Configuration > SCEP certificate
  • One profile per platform · paste the SCEP URI
  • Tenant limit : plan the hierarchy
  • Six CAs maximum per Intune tenant : root CAs, issuing CAs, and BYOCA all count toward the cap, so plan the hierarchy first.
Slide 57

Cloud PKI : the certification authority roles

  • Root CA : trust anchor
  • RSA-4096 · SHA-512
  • 25-year validity recommended for the anchor
  • Issuing CA : signs device certs
  • Built-in SCEP service
  • 5-year validity · inherits root encryption
  • Key storage : TPM or software KSP
  • Private key created on the device
  • Never leaves it · CSR signed by the issuing CA
  • BYOCA : bring your own root
  • Chain an Intune issuing CA
  • To an existing on-prem or third-party root CA
Slide 58

Automate certificate issuance and renewal

  • The SCEP lifecycle : from check-in to automatic renewal.
  • 01 Enroll · profile
  • Device checks in : Intune pushes the assigned SCEP profile
  • 02 Generate · CSR
  • Private key created on device, never leaves it; CSR + signed challenge
  • 03 Validate · SCEP RA
  • RA confirms the device is enrolled, managed, and untampered
  • 04 Issue · issuing CA
  • Issuing CA signs the CSR : cert delivered; auto-renews near threshold
  • Validity
  • 1-year default in the SCEP profile
  • Shorter periods (90 days) limit exposure
  • Renewal threshold
  • 20% default
  • Raise to 30-40% for mobile or remote devices that check in less often
  • Revocation
  • CRL valid 7 days · refreshed every 3.5 days
  • Retire or wipe auto-revokes
Slide 59

Monitor certificate health and compliance

  • Three states plus one common failure : one dashboard catches issues before users feel them.
  • State
  • Signal
  • What it means
  • Next move
  • Active
  • Within validity
  • Cert valid · relying parties trust it
  • Monitor expiry
  • Expired
  • Past validity date
  • Device missed the renewal window · auth fails
  • Lower threshold
  • Revoked
  • On the CRL
  • Manual revoke, wipe, or retire · no longer trusted
  • Confirm intent
  • Missing
  • Not delivered
  • SCEP arrived before the trust chain · validation fails
  • Re-check trust profile

Kenniscontrole

Kies per vraag één antwoord. Je kunt opnieuw proberen; de score bepaalt je modulevoortgang niet.

1. Wat is de primaire rol van een issuing CA?

2. Een certificaat is uitgegeven maar Wi-Fi weigert toegang. Wat moet je ook controleren?

Oorspronkelijke kennisvragen uit de cursus (Engels)

Unchanged question wording from the source decks, apart from typography. Answers and explanations, where present, come from the associated speaker notes. Treat them as course-source answers; validate current product behavior in Microsoft documentation.

Slide 60 · Question 1

Contoso wants to deploy Microsoft Cloud PKI. Their IT team needs to decide how many certification authorities (CAs) to create. What is the maximum number of CAs allowed in a single Intune tenant?

  1. 2
  2. 6
  3. 10
Answer and source explanation

Q1 - Correct: B. 6 Correct. Microsoft Cloud PKI allows a maximum of six CAs per Intune tenant, counting root CAs, issuing CAs, and Bring Your Own CA (BYOCA) instances combined. A. Incorrect. The tenant limit is higher than two. Cloud PKI allows up to six CAs across root, issuing, and Bring Your Own CA (BYOCA) instances combined. C. Incorrect. Ten exceeds the tenant limit. Cloud PKI caps the total CA count at six per Intune tenant.

Slide 60 · Question 2

An administrator at Fabrikam configures Cloud PKI and creates a root CA and an issuing CA. Devices receive the SCEP certificate profile but are rejecting the issued certificates. What is the most likely cause?

  1. The issuing CA validity period is set to more than 5 years.
  2. The trusted certificate profiles for the root CA and issuing CA weren't deployed before the SCEP profile.
  3. The renewal threshold was set to 20%, which is too low for initial enrollment.
Answer and source explanation

Q2 - Correct: B. The trusted certificate profiles for the root CA and issuing CA weren't deployed before the SCEP profile. Correct. Devices must receive and install the trust chain before the SCEP profile arrives. If the SCEP profile is delivered first, the device has no basis to trust the issued certificate and will reject it. Always deploy trusted certificate profiles before deploying SCEP profiles. A. Incorrect. Validity period doesn't determine whether a device accepts a certificate. The issue is almost always that the device doesn't yet trust the CA chain. C. Incorrect. The renewal threshold only affects when renewal triggers : it doesn't affect initial issuance or whether the device accepts the certificate.

Slide 60 · Question 3

A security engineer at Northwind asks: 'Where does the private key get created during SCEP-based certificate enrollment?' What is the correct answer?

  1. On the Cloud PKI SCEP service in Azure
  2. On the device itself; it never leaves the device
  3. On the issuing CA, and then delivered securely to the device
Answer and source explanation

Q3 - Correct: B. On the device itself; it never leaves the device Correct. A fundamental security property of SCEP enrollment is that the device generates the private key locally and submits only the public key in the certificate signing request (CSR). The private key is never transmitted to any server. A. Incorrect. The SCEP service receives the certificate signing request (CSR) and the public key, but never the private key. The private key is generated and stays on the device. C. Incorrect. The issuing CA only signs the public key contained in the CSR : it never generates or holds the device's private key.

Slide 61 · Question 1

Contoso's SCEP certificate profiles are configured with a one-year validity period and a 20% renewal threshold. Several remote devices that connect to Intune infrequently show expired certificates. What is the best remediation?

  1. Increase the certificate validity period to 5 years so certificates last longer.
  2. Increase the renewal threshold to 40% and shorten the validity period to 180 days to extend the renewal window.
  3. Manually revoke and reissue certificates for all affected devices each month.
Answer and source explanation

Q4 - Correct: B. Increase the renewal threshold to 40% and shorten the validity period to 180 days to extend the renewal window. Correct. For devices that check in infrequently, a higher renewal threshold combined with a shorter validity period creates a longer absolute renewal window. For example, a 40% threshold on a 180-day certificate starts renewal 72 days before expiry: giving the device more time to connect and renew before the certificate expires. A. Incorrect. Longer validity weakens security and doesn't fix the underlying problem : devices still won't renew if they're offline when the renewal threshold triggers. C. Incorrect. Manual revocation and reissue doesn't scale and defeats the automation Cloud PKI is designed to provide. Tune the validity and renewal threshold so devices renew automatically.

Slide 61 · Question 2

An administrator needs to verify that a specific Windows device received a certificate and that the subject name and SAN values are correct. Which built-in tool can they use directly on the device?

  1. The Cloud PKI dashboard in the Intune admin center
  2. certmgr.msc or certutil -store My run on the device
  3. Microsoft Entra sign-in logs
Answer and source explanation

Q5 - Correct: B. `certmgr.msc` or `certutil -store My` run on the device Correct. Certificate Manager (`certmgr.msc`) and the `certutil -store My` command-line tool run locally on the Windows device and show the actual certificate, including its subject, SAN values, and issuer chain. A. Incorrect. The dashboard shows certificate metadata reported by Intune, but you can't inspect the full certificate (subject, SAN, issuer chain) from the admin center. C. Incorrect. Sign-in logs surface authentication outcomes : they don't show certificate subject or SAN content on the device.

Learning Path 05

Automatiseren en optimaliseren

3 modules · Nederlands met Engelstalige cursusverdieping

Learning Path 05Module 01

Automatiseren met PowerShell en Microsoft Graph

Je automatiseert Intune-beheer via gecontroleerde API-toegang en herhaalbare scripts.

← Vorige moduleVolgende module →↑ Terug naar boven

Waarom is deze module belangrijk?

Automatisering kan veel apparaten tegelijk raken. Identiteit, bevoegdheden, logging en foutafhandeling bepalen de betrouwbaarheid.

Leerdoelen

  • Uitleggen: de juiste uitvoeringsplaats.
  • Uitleggen: aPI-toegang beveiligen.
  • Uitleggen: aanroepen robuust maken.
  • Uitleggen: clientscripts en governance.

01. De juiste uitvoeringsplaats

Maak onderscheid tussen een beheerscript dat Graph aanroept en een script dat via Intune op de client draait. Het eerste verandert cloudobjecten; het tweede werkt in een lokale gebruikers- of systeemcontext. Kies de kleinste uitvoeringsscope die het probleem oplost en leg afhankelijkheden vast.

02. API-toegang beveiligen

Een appregistratie beschrijft de toepassing en haar toegang. Delegated permissions werken namens een gebruiker; application permissions gebruiken een toepassingsidentiteit. Vraag alleen noodzakelijke rechten en de vereiste consent. Bescherm credentials, kies waar passend certificaten of managed identities en voorkom secrets in scripts of logs.

03. Aanroepen robuust maken

Controleer de gebruikte Graph-versie en beschikbare Intune-endpoints. Verwerk paginering, throttling, retries en foutcodes. Gebruik een leescontrole vóór een wijziging en maak acties waar mogelijk idempotent. Een beta-endpoint kan veranderen en verdient extra aandacht in productieautomatisering.

04. Clientscripts en governance

De Intune Management Extension voert ondersteunde scripts en workloads uit op Windows. Configureer context en architectuur bewust. Test eerst op een pilot, log zonder gevoelige inhoud en bied een herstelroute. Houd code en goedkeuringen in versiebeheer en monitor het daadwerkelijke resultaat na uitvoering.

Wat moet je als beheerder onthouden?

  • Cloud-API en lokale scripts zijn verschillende uitvoeringsmodellen.
  • Beperk API-rechten en bescherm credentials.
  • Verwerk paginering, throttling en gedeeltelijke fouten.

Samenvatting

Cloud-API en lokale scripts zijn verschillende uitvoeringsmodellen. Beperk API-rechten en bescherm credentials. Verwerk paginering, throttling en gedeeltelijke fouten. Gebruik de hoofdstukken om keuzes te onderbouwen en controleer altijd de effectieve toestand naast de portalstatus.

Oorspronkelijke kennisvragen uit de cursus (Engels)

Unchanged question wording from the source decks, apart from typography. Answers and explanations, where present, come from the associated speaker notes. Treat them as course-source answers; validate current product behavior in Microsoft documentation.

Slide 13 · Question 1

You're setting up an Entra ID app registration that an unattended Azure Function will use to query Intune-managed devices through Microsoft Graph. Which authentication model and permission type should you choose?

  1. App-only access with application permissions (for example, DeviceManagementManagedDevices.Read.All).
  2. Delegated access with delegated permissions, signed in as the Intune Administrator each run.
  3. Anonymous access : Microsoft Graph allows read-only Intune queries without authentication.
Answer and source explanation

Q1 - Correct: A. App-only access with application permissions (for example, `DeviceManagementManagedDevices.Read.All`). Correct. Unattended automation should use app-only access with application permissions so the service can run without a signed-in user. Application permissions are the supported pattern for app-only Intune Graph access. B. Delegated permissions require an interactive user sign-in, which doesn't fit unattended automation in an Azure Function. C. Microsoft Graph never permits anonymous access to Intune data. Every call must be authenticated.

Slide 13 · Question 2

Which credential type is the most appropriate for an Azure Function or container that authenticates to Microsoft Graph for Intune automation?

  1. A long-lived client secret stored as an environment variable in the function code.
  2. A managed identity (when the hosting service supports it) : no app secret has to be stored or rotated.
  3. The Intune Administrator's user password embedded in the script.
Answer and source explanation

Q2 - Correct: B. A managed identity (when the hosting service supports it) : no app secret has to be stored or rotated. Correct. For Azure Functions, Azure Automation, and similar Azure-hosted workloads, a managed identity is the recommended option because there's no secret to store, leak, or rotate. A. Long-lived client secrets stored in code or plain environment variables are the weakest option : they expire silently and risk being leaked. Use a managed identity, federated credentials, or a certificate from Key Vault. C. Embedding any user's credentials in a script is a serious security anti-pattern and isn't compatible with app-only Graph automation.

Slide 13 · Question 3

You deploy a PowerShell script to Windows devices through Intune. After assignment, when does the Intune Management Extension run the script again on a successfully completed device?

  1. Every hour, automatically, until you remove the assignment.
  2. Only when the script content changes or the assignment changes : scripts run once by default and don't rerun automatically otherwise.
  3. Every time the user signs in, regardless of script outcome.
Answer and source explanation

Q3 - Correct: B. Only when the script content changes or the assignment changes : scripts run once by default and don't rerun automatically otherwise. Correct. Intune PowerShell scripts run once by default. The IME reruns them only when the script content is updated or the assignment changes. The IME does retry failed runs up to three times. A. The IME checks for new or changed scripts every hour, but a successfully run script doesn't rerun on the same schedule. C. Script execution isn't tied to user sign-in events for already-successful scripts.

Slide 14 · Question 1

A scripted automation needs to configure a new Windows compliance policy that lives in the modern Settings Catalog. Which Microsoft Graph endpoint should it target?

  1. /deviceManagement/deviceConfigurations : the standard endpoint for any modern device configuration.
  2. /deviceManagement/configurationPolicies : the endpoint for Settings Catalog policies.
  3. /deviceManagement/managedDevices : the endpoint for device inventory and configuration.
Answer and source explanation

Q4 - Correct: B. `/deviceManagement/configurationPolicies` : the endpoint for Settings Catalog policies. Correct. Settings Catalog policies are managed via `deviceManagement/configurationPolicies`. The legacy `deviceConfigurations` endpoint is reserved for template-based profiles. A. `deviceManagement/deviceConfigurations` is the legacy template-based endpoint. It should be used only to manage existing legacy profiles, not for new Settings Catalog policies. C. `managedDevices` exposes device inventory and per-device operations, not policy definitions.

Slide 14 · Question 2

You need to find every Intune-managed device that is currently not compliant so you can drive a remediation workflow. Which Graph query expresses this efficiently?

  1. GET /deviceManagement/deviceCompliancePolicies and inspect each policy's settings client-side.
  2. GET /deviceManagement/managedDevices?$filter=complianceState ne 'compliant'
  3. GET /users and check each user's assignedLicenses for Intune entitlement.
Answer and source explanation

Q5 - Correct: B. `GET /deviceManagement/managedDevices?$filter=complianceState ne 'compliant'` Correct. Filtering `managedDevices` with `complianceState ne 'compliant'` returns the set of noncompliant devices directly from Graph, which is the most efficient way to drive a remediation workflow. A. Listing compliance policies doesn't tell you which devices are noncompliant : that's per-device state, not policy definitions. C. User license lookup doesn't return device compliance state : Intune compliance lives on the device object, not on the user object.
